# RTX Options

This file contains a complete reference of all configurable RTX Options in RTX Remix.

For detailed documentation on the RtxOption system architecture, including layers, priorities, and how values are resolved, see [RemixConfig.md](documentation/RemixConfig.md).

This file is generated from the source by `scripts/gen_rtx_options_md.py`. Run `python scripts/gen_rtx_options_md.py` after adding or changing an RTX option, and commit the result.

## Simple Types
| RTX Option | Type | Default Value | Min Value | Max Value | Description |
| :-- | :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: | :-- |
|rtx.accel.autoCellSize|bool|True|||When rtx\.mergedBucketCellSize is 0, derives the merged BLAS cell size from rtx\.sceneScale, as about 58\.5 metres in the game's world units\. Keeps merged BLAS cluster rebuilds small in any game without a hand\-tuned cell size, for API hosts and D3D9 games alike\. An explicit rtx\.mergedBucketCellSize takes precedence\.|
|rtx.accel.clusterFoldsPerFrame|int|2|||The most cluster keys per frame whose young buckets join their cluster\. Limits the rebuild cost per frame\.|
|rtx.accel.clusterYoungFrames|int|30|||Merged BLAS instances form stable clusters: instances with the same key \(TLAS class and rtx\.mergedBucketCellSize cell\) always form the same buckets, filled in a fixed order\. Instances created within this many frames stay in buckets of their own, so streaming does not rebuild their cell's cluster every frame\. 0 = off\.|
|rtx.accel.originTranslation|bool|False|||With rtx\.scene\.originShiftEvent: when the scene origin shifts, moves the scene's instances without rebuilding their BLASes, and moves cached merged BLASes through their TLAS instance\. This avoids rebuilding every merged bucket in one frame on each origin shift\. Merged\-bucket cells \(rtx\.mergedBucketCellSize\) use absolute coordinates, so clusters keep their keys across shifts\.|
|rtx.accel.tlasRefit|bool|True|||Updates \(refits\) each TLAS instead of rebuilding it while its instance list keeps the same length, order and BLASes, so only transforms and masks change\. A full build still happens every rtx\.accel\.tlasRefitRebuildFrames frames and when more than rtx\.accel\.tlasRefitMovedFraction of the instances moved since the last full build\. Saves TLAS build time without changing the image\.|
|rtx.accel.tlasRefitMovedFraction|float|0.25|||With rtx\.accel\.tlasRefit: a TLAS is fully built again when more than this fraction \(0 to 1\) of its instances moved since its last full build\.|
|rtx.accel.tlasRefitRebuildFrames|int|16|||With rtx\.accel\.tlasRefit: the most consecutive refits of a TLAS before it is fully built again\. Higher values save more build time but let the tree degrade further while instances move\.|
|rtx.accelDirtyLogTopN|int|0|||Diagnostic, with rtx\.accelMemoryLogIntervalFrames: adds an \[AccelDirty\] log line naming the meshes that dirtied the most merged buckets, with the reasons, and an \[AccelSpatial\] line with the merged buckets' extent and overlap\. Costs CPU time while on\. 0 = off, otherwise the number of meshes to list\.|
|rtx.accelIncrementalSelfCheckFrames|int|0|||Diagnostic for the incremental acceleration\-structure options \(rtx\.incrementalBucketBookkeeping, rtx\.parallelDynamicBlasPass, rtx\.ommHoldBoundMicromaps and others\): every this many frames, rebuilds their state from scratch and compares it with the incremental state\. Mismatches are logged as \[AccelIncCheck\], \[DynamicEmitCheck\] or \[OmmBindingCheck\]\. 0 = off\.|
|rtx.accelMemoryLogIntervalFrames|int|300|||Diagnostic: every this many frames, logs an \[AccelMem\] breakdown of acceleration\-structure memory \(geometry copies, merged and per\-geometry BLASes, pooled BLASes, scratch and TLAS\) and of GPU memory fragmentation\. 0 = off\.|
|rtx.accumulation.blendMode|int|0|||The blend mode to use for accumulating debug view output\.<br>Supported modes are: 0 = Average, 1 = Min, 2 = Max\.<br>Average is the default mode and is the most common mode to use for accumulation\.<br>Min and Max are useful for visualizing the minimum or maximum value of a debug view output over time\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_ACCUMULATION\_BLEND\_MODE|
|rtx.accumulation.numberOfFramesToAccumulate|int|1024|1||Number of frames to accumulate render output\.<br>This can be used for generating reference images smoothed over time\.<br>By default the accumulation stops once the limit is reached\.<br>When desired, continous accumulation can be enabled via enableContinuousAccumulation\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_ACCUMULATION\_NUMBER\_OF\_FRAMES\_TO\_ACCUMULATE|
|rtx.accumulation.resetOnCameraTransformChange|bool|True|||Resets the accumulated debug view output when the camera transform changes\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_ACCUMULATION\_RESET\_ON\_CAMERA\_TRANFORM\_CHANGE|
|rtx.adaptiveAccumulation|bool|True|||Environment variable: DXVK\_USE\_ADAPTIVE\_ACCUMULATION|
|rtx.adaptiveResolutionDenoising|bool|True||||
|rtx.adaptiveResolutionReservedGPUMemoryGiB|int|2|||The amount of GPU memory in gibibytes to reserve away from consideration for adaptive resolution replacement textures\.<br>This value should only be changed to reflect the estimated amount of memory Remix itself consumes on the GPU \(aside from texture loading, mostly from rendering\-related buffers\) and should not be changed otherwise\.<br>Only relevant when force high resolution replacement textures is disabled and adaptive resolution replacement textures is enabled\. See asset estimated size parameter for more information\.<br>|
|rtx.advanceTime|bool|True|||A flag to enable or disable advancing time used by Remix subsystems \(particle effects, animations, etc\.\)\.<br><br>Environment variable: RTX\_ADVANCE\_TIME|
|rtx.aliasing.beginPass|int|0|||The first render pass where the aliasing resource is bound in a frame\.|
|rtx.aliasing.depth|int|1|||The depth of the aliasing resource\. Required for 3D textures\.|
|rtx.aliasing.endPass|int|41|||The last render pass where the aliasing resource is bound in a frame\.|
|rtx.aliasing.extentType|int|0|||Specifies the resolution type for the aliasing resource\. If a 3D texture is used, depth must also be set\.|
|rtx.aliasing.formatCategory|int|-1|||Specifies the texture format compatibility category for the aliasing resource\.|
|rtx.aliasing.height|int|720|||The height of the aliasing resource in pixels\.|
|rtx.aliasing.imageType|int|1|||The image type of the aliasing resource \(e\.g\., 1D, 2D, or 3D\)\.|
|rtx.aliasing.imageViewType|int|1|||The image view type of the aliasing resource \(e\.g\., 1D, 2D, 3D, or cube\)\.|
|rtx.aliasing.layer|int|1|||The number of layers in the aliasing resource\.|
|rtx.aliasing.width|int|1280|||The width of the aliasing resource in pixels\.|
|rtx.allowCubemaps|bool|False|||When enabled, cubemaps from the game are processed through Remix, but they may not render correctly\.|
|rtx.allowFSE|bool|False|||A flag indicating if the application should be able to utilize exclusive full screen mode when set to true, otherwise force it to be disabled when set to false\.<br>Exclusive full screen may see performance benefits over other fullscreen modes at the cost of stability in some cases\.<br>Do note that on modern Windows full screen optimizations will likely be used regardless which in most cases results in performance similar to exclusive full screen even when it is not in use\.|
|rtx.alphaBake.census|bool|False|||Diagnostic: before a baked opacity micromap is freed, classifies each of its triangles as fully transparent, fully opaque or mixed, and logs the totals as \[AlphaBake\] every 300 frames\. Costs one small compute dispatch per micromap\. Does not change rendering\.|
|rtx.alphaBake.censusMicroStates|bool|False|||Diagnostic, with rtx\.alphaBake\.census: also counts the micro\-triangle states \(transparent, opaque, unknown\-transparent and unknown\-opaque\) of each baked micromap, by format and subdivision level, and adds them to the \[AlphaBake\] lines\. Costs one more small compute dispatch per micromap\. Does not change rendering\.|
|rtx.alphaBake.softwareOmm|int|0|0|2|Software opacity micromaps, for GPUs without hardware micromap support\. 0 = off\. 1 = only when the device lacks VK\_EXT\_opacity\_micromap \(or rtx\.alphaBake\.softwareOmmSimulateNoExtension is on\)\. 2 = always, without hardware micromaps, for testing on GPUs that have them\.<br>The micromaps are baked as for hardware and stored in GPU buffers that the shaders read\. Needs rtx\.opacityMicromap\.enable\. They have no effect until a consumer option such as rtx\.alphaBake\.softwareOmmVisibility is on\. \[SoftOmm\] log lines report them every 300 frames\.|
|rtx.alphaBake.softwareOmmCounters|bool|False|||Diagnostic: counts visibility\-ray candidates by software micromap state per pass and logs them as \[SoftOmm\] counters every 300 frames\. Works without rtx\.alphaBake\.softwareOmmVisibility too\. Costs GPU time\.|
|rtx.alphaBake.softwareOmmResolveSkip|bool|False|||The primary and PSR ray passes use the software opacity micromaps \(rtx\.alphaBake\.softwareOmm\) as hardware micromaps are used: a hit on a transparent micro\-triangle is skipped without an alpha test and without using up an interaction, and unknown micro\-triangles are alpha tested as usual\. After 16 such skips a ray falls back to the normal alpha test\. rtx\.alphaBake\.softwareOmmResolveSkipIndirect extends this to the indirect passes\.|
|rtx.alphaBake.softwareOmmResolveSkipIndirect|bool|False|||Also applies rtx\.alphaBake\.softwareOmmResolveSkip to the indirect passes, including the SHARC update and query passes\. Needs rtx\.alphaBake\.softwareOmmResolveSkip\. This can cost more GPU time in the indirect passes than it saves\.|
|rtx.alphaBake.softwareOmmSelfTest|bool|False|||Testing: runs a compute pass that checks the software micromap lookup against the bake for every micro\-triangle of subdivision levels 0\-12, and logs \[SoftOmm\] self\-test lines\. Turn it off and on again to repeat it\. Needs no micromaps\.|
|rtx.alphaBake.softwareOmmSimulateNoExtension|bool|False|||Testing: treats VK\_EXT\_opacity\_micromap as unsupported, as on a GPU without the extension\. With rtx\.alphaBake\.softwareOmm = 1, software micromaps then take over\. Takes effect after a restart\.|
|rtx.alphaBake.softwareOmmTraversal|int|0|0|7|Skips transparent software micromap micro\-triangles inside ray traversal instead of re\-tracing past them, per ray query pass\. A bitmask: 1 = G\-buffer \(primary and PSR\), 2 = SHARC update, 4 = indirect integration \(ray query mode only, rtx\.renderPassIntegrateIndirectRaytraceMode = 0\)\. Needs rtx\.alphaBake\.softwareOmm\. In the selected passes it takes the place of rtx\.alphaBake\.softwareOmmResolveSkip\. On GPUs with fixed\-function traversal, every non\-opaque triangle a ray passes costs extra time\.|
|rtx.alphaBake.softwareOmmVisibility|bool|False|||Shadow and light\-sampling visibility rays use the software opacity micromaps \(rtx\.alphaBake\.softwareOmm\): transparent micro\-triangles are skipped, opaque ones are accepted without an alpha test, and unknown ones are alpha tested as usual, as with hardware micromaps\. Saves alpha tests on alpha\-tested geometry\.|
|rtx.alwaysCopyDecalGeometries|bool|True|||When set to True tells the geometry processor to always copy decals geometry\. This is an optimization flag to experiment with when rtx\.useBuffersDirectly is True\.|
|rtx.alwaysWaitForAsyncTextures|bool|False|||Force CPU to wait for the texture upload\. Do not use an asynchronous thread for textures\. If true, a frame stutter should be expected\.<br>Environment variable: DXVK\_WAIT\_ASYNC\_TEXTURES|
|rtx.antiCulling.light.enable|bool|False|||Enable Anti\-Culling for lights\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_ANTI\_CULLING\_LIGHTS|
|rtx.antiCulling.light.fovScale|float|1|||Scalar of the FOV of lights Anti\-Culling Frustum\.|
|rtx.antiCulling.light.numFramesToExtendLightLifetime|int|1000|||Maximum number of frames to keep  when Anti\-Culling is enabled\. Make sure not to set this too low \(then the anti\-culling won't work\), nor too high \(which will hurt the performance\)\.|
|rtx.antiCulling.light.numLightsToKeep|int|1000|||\(DEPRECATED\)|
|rtx.antiCulling.object.enable|bool|False|||Extends lifetime of objects that go outside the camera frustum \(anti\-culling frustum\)\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_ANTI\_CULLING\_OBJECTS|
|rtx.antiCulling.object.enableHighPrecisionAntiCulling|bool|True|||Use robust intersection check with Separate Axis Theorem\.<br>This method is slightly expensive but it effectively addresses object flickering issues that arise from corner cases in the fast intersection check method\.<br>Typically, it's advisable to enable this option unless it results in a notable performance drop; otherwise, the presence of flickering artifacts could significantly diminish the overall image quality\.|
|rtx.antiCulling.object.enableInfinityFarFrustum|bool|False|||Enable infinity far plane frustum for anti\-culling\.|
|rtx.antiCulling.object.farPlaneScale|float|10|||Scale applied to the far plane for Anti\-Culling Frustum for matching the culling frustum in the original game\.|
|rtx.antiCulling.object.fovScale|float|1|||Scale applied to the FOV of Anti\-Culling Frustum for matching the culling frustum in the original game\.|
|rtx.antiCulling.object.hashInstanceWithBoundingBoxHash|bool|True|||Hash instances with bounding box hash for object duplication check\.<br> Disable this when the game using primitive culling which may cause flickering\.|
|rtx.antiCulling.object.numObjectsToKeep|int|10000|||The maximum number of RayTracing instances to keep when Anti\-Culling is enabled\.|
|rtx.apiRecord.enable|bool|False|||Records every Remix API call the host makes, from device registration until shutdown or the Stop Recording button \(Developer \> Performance Logging \> API Recording\), into a new recording\-YYYYMMDD\-HHMMSS folder that tools/remix\_replay can play back\. Takes effect after a restart\.<br>D3D11 texture imports are copied and saved as DDS files, and mesh and texture data are written on the calling thread, so expect hitches while recording\. Recordings contain the game's assets, so keep them private\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_API\_RECORD|
|rtx.applicationId|int|102100511|||Used to uniquely identify the application to DLSS\. Generally should not be changed without good reason\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspective|bool|True|||Draws the air between the camera and every surface: haze, fog, sun shafts and the glow around lamps\. Near the camera it uses a scene\-shadowed froxel grid, and beyond that an unshadowed far\-field table\. This is the strongest distance cue in an outdoor scene\. With fog and local lights on, it can replace the renderer's own volumetric fog\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveDebugView|int|0|0|10|Haze debug view: replaces each pixel with one measurement of the haze, to find where its light comes from\. A strip along the bottom of the screen shows the scale, so compare a pixel with the strip rather than with how bright it looks\. 0: Off\. 1: Sun Visibility at the pixel's depth \(gray, white = sees the sun; small values below 0\.1 show in red, stretched ten times, so a small leak is not mistaken for none\)\. 2: Sky Visibility at the pixel's depth\. 3: Haze Brightness, the brightness the haze adds at the pixel in cd/m^2, in false color from 0\.0001 \(dark blue\) to 10000 \(white\) with a dark line at every power of ten\. 4: No Sun or Sky Light, the normal image with the near\-field haze lit as if nothing saw the sun or the sky\. 5: Haze Transmittance \(white = clear\)\. 6: Sun Visibility 2 m from the camera\. 7: Sky Visibility 2 m from the camera\. 8: Haze Brightness with no sun or sky light \(3 and 4 together\)\. 9: Surface Clamp, the share of the haze samples moved in front of the surface the game's depth shows \(white = all\)\. 10: Surface Guard, the share moved further in front because the game's depth lay behind the visible geometry, as with displaced surfaces, mirrors and glass\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveDepthRangeMeters|float|32000|100||Deprecated: the far field always reaches the horizon, and the near field's reach is rtx\.atmosphere\.aerialPerspectiveFroxelRangeMeters\. Ignored\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveFroxelDepthSlices|int|96|16|256|The number of depth slices in the near\-field haze, spread from the camera to Froxel Range\. More slices give finer detail in depth\. GPU cost and memory grow linearly with it\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveFroxelRangeMeters|float|1000|10|50000|How far the near\-field haze reaches from the camera, in metres\. Inside it the air is shadowed by the scene \(sun shafts, dark interiors\), lit by local lights and carries the height fog\. Beyond it, an unshadowed far field carries the atmosphere to the horizon\. Raising it spreads the same slices over a longer distance\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveFroxelSizePixels|int|8|2|64|The screen size of one froxel of the near\-field haze, in render\-resolution pixels\. 8 gives a 240x135 grid at 1080p\. Smaller froxels sharpen sun shafts and lamp glow\. GPU cost and memory grow with 1 / size squared\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveFroxelUnitsPerMeter|float|0|0||Deprecated: the fog, local\-light glow and froxel range now share rtx\.atmosphere\.worldScale with the clouds\. A value that disagrees with the clouds' scale is reported, and the clouds' scale is kept\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveHistoryFrames|int|32|1|255|How many frames of history each near\-field froxel averages\. Each frame a froxel traces one sun\-or\-sky visibility ray and one local\-light ray, and the history smooths them into stable shadowing\. More frames look smoother, but a moving light or occluder takes longer to settle\. Camera cuts reset the history\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveIntegrateScan|bool|True|||Deprecated and ignored\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveLocalLightCutoff|float|0.002|0||The glow brightness below which a light counts as not reaching a point\. Each light's reach is sized from its own power, so a torch stops after a few metres and a floodlight reaches much further\. Lower it if bright lights visibly stop lighting the fog at a fixed distance\. Raise it to save GPU time\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveLocalLightIntensity|float|1|0|100|A gain on the glow of local lights in the haze\. 1 is physical\. Raise it when a game's lights are authored dimmer than the haze that looks right at a distance\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveLocalLightMaxCount|int|256|0|4096|The maximum number of scene lights the near\-field haze uses per frame\. The lights that can glow brightest in view are kept\. Lower it to save GPU time in scenes with very many lights\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveLocalLightShadowRangeMeters|float|300|0||Deprecated: local lights are shadowed throughout the near field \(rtx\.atmosphere\.aerialPerspectiveFroxelRangeMeters\)\. Ignored\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveLocalLightShadows|bool|True|||Traces a shadow ray from each froxel toward the local light it sampled\. Without it, a lamp lights the fog on both sides of the wall it stands behind\. With it, light through a doorway casts a real shaft\. Needs ray\-traced scene geometry\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveLocalLightUnitsPerMeter|float|0|0||Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.worldScale, which scales the local\-light glow with everything else\. A value that disagrees with the clouds' scale is reported, and the clouds' scale is kept\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveLocalLightWindow|bool|False|||Fades each local light's glow smoothly to zero at its cull radius, instead of letting it fall off with distance and stop abruptly at that radius\. Turn it on for games whose lights are authored with such a falloff window\. Local Light Cutoff then sets the reach\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveLocalLights|bool|True|||Lets scene lights such as lamps, torches, muzzle flashes and headlights glow in the near\-field haze, alongside the sun and sky\. The cost follows how many lights are near each other on screen, not the total light count\. With this on, the renderer's own volumetric lighting can be turned off\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveLutDepthSlices|int|32|4|128|Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.aerialPerspectiveFroxelDepthSlices\. Ignored\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveLutResolution|int|192|8|256|Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.aerialPerspectiveFroxelSizePixels\. Ignored\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveMieAnisotropyMax|float|0.8|-1|1|The upper limit on how strongly the haze scatters light forward, used by the haze in front of surfaces but not by the sky\. A strongly forward\-scattering haze is brightest looking toward the sun, where the far field cannot know that the air is in shadow\. Lower it if the haze glows too much toward a sun hidden behind scenery\. Values at or above Mie Anisotropy have no effect\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveNearFadeEndMeters|float|250|0||Deprecated and ignored: the near\-field haze needs no near fade\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveNearFadeStartMeters|float|50|0||Deprecated and ignored: the near\-field haze needs no near fade\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveScale|float|0|||Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.unitsPerMeter\. An existing value is migrated automatically\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveSceneShadow|bool|True|||Traces the scene to find how much of the sun and the sky each near\-field froxel sees\. This gives sun shafts through doorways and foliage, and keeps interiors from filling with sunlit haze\. Needs ray\-traced scene geometry\. Without it the near field is unshadowed\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveSceneShadowDebug|int|0|0|2|Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.aerialPerspectiveDebugView\. Ignored\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveSceneShadowRangeMeters|float|4600|0||Deprecated: the near field is shadowed throughout its range \(rtx\.atmosphere\.aerialPerspectiveFroxelRangeMeters\)\. Ignored\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveSeparateVisibility|bool|True|||Deprecated and ignored\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveVisibilityPerSlice|bool|True|||Deprecated and ignored\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveVisibilityUpdatePeriod|int|1|1|8|Deprecated: the near field spreads its rays over its history \(rtx\.atmosphere\.aerialPerspectiveHistoryFrames\)\. Ignored\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerialPerspectiveWorldCompression|float|1|0.1||Deprecated: the haze now shares rtx\.atmosphere\.worldScale with the clouds\. A value that disagrees with the clouds' scale is reported, and the clouds' scale is kept\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerosolDensity|float|1.1|||A multiplier on the density of dust and haze particles\. 1 is typical\. Higher values give a hazier, whiter sky and a larger glow around the sun\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.aerosolTint|float3|1, 1, 1|||A per\-channel multiplier on the haze's scattering and absorption together\. 1, 1, 1 leaves the haze as configured\. Lowering a channel makes the haze affect that colour less, so 1, 0\.8, 0\.55 gives a warm dust storm\. Weather and game integrations can set it\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.airDensity|float|1|||A multiplier on the density of air molecules\. 1 is a clear Earth sky\. Higher values give a deeper blue sky and redder sunsets\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.airTint|float3|1, 1, 1|||A per\-channel multiplier on the air's scattering\. 1, 1, 1 is Earth's air\. Change it for an alien or polluted sky colour\. Weather can blend it\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.altitudeScale|float|1|||Deprecated: a vertical\-only scale is no longer supported\. Set rtx\.atmosphere\.unitsPerMeter to the game's true scale instead\. A value other than 1 is reported and ignored\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.atmosphereThickness|float|100|||The thickness of the atmosphere, in kilometres\. 100 is Earth\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.calendarYear|int|2026|1900|2100|The year of the date the sky is drawn for, used with Day of Year and Time of Day\. The real moon's position and phase, and the stars, follow it\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cameraWorldOverride|float3|0, 0, 0|||The camera's world position in the game's own units and axis convention, used as the cloud anchor while Use Camera World Override is on\. A game integration normally sends it every frame\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudAltitude|float|1.3|||Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.cloudBaseHeightMeters\. An existing value is migrated automatically\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudAnchorCutKm|float|0.5|||How far, in kilometres, the cloud anchor has to jump in one frame to count as a camera cut, which rebuilds the cloud reflection dome at once\. Teleports and loading into a new area move it further than a player can travel in one frame\. 0 turns the check off\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudBaseHeightMeters|float|1300|||The height of the main cloud deck's underside above the ground level, in metres\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudBodyBillowGain|float|0.65|0|1|The strength of each finer billow size relative to the one above it\. 0\.5 lets the large billows dominate, for smooth lobes\. Toward 0\.8, small billows are nearly as strong as large ones, for a cauliflower look\. Changing it rebuilds the cloud shape field\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudBodyBillowSizeKm|float|1.2|0.2|4|The size of the largest billows, in kilometres\. Three finer sizes follow at a half, a quarter and an eighth of it\. Keep the finest above about 0\.1 km\. Changing it rebuilds the cloud shape field\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudBodyBillowStrength|float|2|0|3|How far the billows push the cloud surface in and out, as a multiple of Billow Size\. 0 gives smooth towers\. Changing it rebuilds the cloud shape field\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudBoilSpeed|float|0.002|||How fast cloud detail is sheared downwind, in kilometres per second\. The tops stream downwind while the bases stay put\. 0 turns the shear off\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudCellSizeKm|float|3.65|||The average size of a cloud cluster, in kilometres, from 0\.5 to 6\. Smaller values give many small clouds, larger values fewer and broader banks\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudColor|float3|0.89, 0.92, 1|||The base colour of the clouds\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudColumnBaseVariation|float|0.12|||How much cloud bases rise and fall, as a share of the layer depth, from 0 to 0\.4\. 0 gives perfectly flat bases\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudColumnFeather|float|0.35|||How soft the edges of cloud clusters are, from 0\.05 to 1\. Low values give crisp, solid clouds\. High values give soft, wispy edges\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudColumnTopShape|float|0.4|||How cloud tops rise, from 0\.1 to 2\. Low values let thin cluster edges tower too, for blockier clouds\. High values let only dense cores rise, for domed tops and feathered edges\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudColumnTopVariation|float|0.45|||How much cloud tops vary in height, from 0 to 1\. 0 puts all tops at one height, a flat deck\. Higher values give a varied skyline\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudCoverageMean|float|0.54|||The average share of the sky covered by cloud, from 0 \(clear\) to 1 \(overcast\)\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudCoverageNoiseScale|float|0.00257732|||How large the regions of different coverage are\. Smaller values give larger regions\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudCoverageSpread|float|0|||How much the cloud coverage varies across the sky, from 0 \(the same everywhere\) to 1 \(the full range\)\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudCurlStrengthKm|float|0.12|0|1|How far, in kilometres, the detail is swirled sideways at the cloud base\. It tears the base into wind\-torn wisps and keeps the detail from looking stamped on\. 0 turns it off\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudDensity|float|20|||How dense and opaque the clouds are\. Higher values give darker, more solid clouds\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudDepthMeters|float|2000|||The vertical depth of the main cloud deck, in metres, measured up from its base\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudDetailLodBias|float|-3|||The blur bias used while Cloud Detail LOD Mode is on\. Negative values keep more detail, positive values blur further\. It has no effect while the mode is off\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudDetailLodMode|int|0|||Blurs cloud detail by sample spacing in the cloud march and the reflection dome\. It changes cloud shapes as well as fine texture\.<br>0: Off, the native look\.<br>1: Off, the same as 0\.<br>2: Always on\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudDiffuseSunStrength|float|0|0|1|How much sunlight reaches the shaded parts of clouds after passing through the cloud above them, from 0 to 1\. 0 keeps deep shadows under and between clouds\. 1 lights grey, overcast or stormy skies the way thick cloud passes light, neutral grey rather than blue\. The overcast, drizzle and storm weather presets set 1\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudDiskAureole|bool|True|||Draws the glow \(aureole\) that forms around the sun and the moons behind thin cloud, where cloud droplets bend light a few degrees off its path\. Behind thin cloud the disc becomes a bright glow, and behind thicker cloud only the glow remains\. Off, the disc is only dimmed\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudDropletDiameter|float|20|5|50|The typical cloud droplet size, in micrometres, from 5 to 50\. 20 is typical of stratocumulus\. Smaller droplets, as in fresh cumulus and fog, give a wider glow around the sun and the moons\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudEnabled|bool|True|||Draws the clouds\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudEvolutionSpeed|float|0.002|||How fast cloud detail rises through each cloud, in kilometres per second, which makes cumulus look like it is building\. It does not move the clouds themselves\. 0 stops the rise\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudGroundAlbedo|float|0.2|0|1|Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.groundAlbedo\. An existing value is migrated automatically\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudHexTilingEnable|bool|True|||Hides repetition in the cloud pattern by blending randomly offset, rotated and mirrored copies of it\. Turn it off only to see the plain repeating pattern\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudHistoryDepthTolerance|float|0.1|0|1|How different the distance to the surface behind a cloud pixel may be for the cloud filters to still reuse a sample there, as a share of that distance\. Lower values reject more history next to moving objects, which avoids smearing at the cost of more noise\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudLayer2Albedo|float3|1, 1, 1|0, 0, 0|1, 1, 1|The share of light layer 2's particles scatter rather than absorb, per colour channel\. 1, 1, 1 is water droplets, which absorb nothing\. Lower values absorb, and because light scatters many times the colour shifts far more than the value suggests\. Dust is about 0\.95, 0\.9, 0\.8\. Smoke and soot are lower\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudLayer2Altitude|float|5.5|||Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.cloudLayer2BaseHeightMeters\. An existing value is migrated automatically\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudLayer2BaseHeightMeters|float|5500|||The height of cloud layer 2's base above the ground level, in metres\. 0 or slightly below puts a fog bank on the ground\. Ignored while Layer 2 Follows Camera is on\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudLayer2CameraOffsetMeters|float|-30|||Layer 2's base relative to the camera while Layer 2 Follows Camera is on, in metres\. Negative values put the base below the eye: \-30 with a depth of 60 puts the camera in the middle of the layer\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudLayer2Color|float3|0.89, 0.92, 1|||Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.cloudLayer2Albedo\. Layer 2 uses the main deck's Cloud Color with its own albedo\. An existing value is migrated\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudLayer2CoverageMean|float|0.85|||The share of the area layer 2 covers, from 0 to 1\. The layer is a sheet broken into patches a few kilometres across\. Near 1 they merge into a closed sheet with a few holes\. Independent of the main deck's coverage\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudLayer2DensityScale|float|0.65|||Layer 2's density as a multiple of Cloud Density\. At the default cloud density, 1 gives about 200 m of visibility inside a fog bank and 0\.25 about 800 m\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudLayer2DepthMeters|float|2000|||The vertical depth of cloud layer 2, in metres\. Use 50\-500 for ground fog or a dust layer, and a few hundred to 2000 for stratus or a high deck\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudLayer2Enable|bool|False|||Draws cloud layer 2: a flat layer such as ground fog, stratus, a high deck or dust\. It is lit like the main deck, works at any height \(including on the ground with the camera inside it\) and is cut off by scene geometry\. It shades and is shaded by the main deck when one lies between the other and the sun\. Shadows on the ground and in the air come only from the main deck\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudLayer2FollowCamera|bool|False|||Keeps layer 2's base at the camera's height plus Layer 2 Camera Offset instead of at Layer 2 Base Height, for a dust or fog layer the player always stands in, whatever the terrain height\. Only the height follows the camera\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudLayer2NoiseSeed|float|1000|||The seed for layer 2's patch layout\. Different seeds give unrelated layouts\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudLayer2StepFloor|int|8|||Deprecated and ignored: layer 2 uses the main deck's step settings\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudLayer2StepMax|int|32|||Deprecated and ignored: layer 2 uses the main deck's step limit \(rtx\.atmosphere\.cloudViewSamplesMax\)\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudLayer2Thickness|float|2|||Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.cloudLayer2DepthMeters\. An existing value is migrated automatically\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudLayer2TypeMean|float|0.6|||How layer 2's density changes with height, from 0 to 1\. 0 is stratus or fog, densest at the base and thinning toward a ragged top\. 1 is cumulus\-like, thin at the base and full by mid\-height, with billowy rather than wispy detail\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudLayer2TypeSpread|float|1|||How much layer 2's cloud type varies across the sky, from 0 to 1\. Independent of the main deck\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudLightingLodThreshold|float|0.05|||Saves GPU time by reusing the lighting of nearby cloud samples for samples that barely show, deep inside a cloud or far away, from 0 to 0\.25\. 0 lights every sample\. Raise it for speed\. Too high a value can flatten cloud shading\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudMarchRate|int|0|0|3|How many cloud pixels are marched each frame\. The others are rebuilt from neighbouring samples and the cloud filter's history\.<br>0: Auto, chosen from the render and output resolution: quarter rate at native resolution and mild upscaling with at least 720 render lines, full rate otherwise\.<br>1: Full, every pixel every frame\.<br>2: Quarter, one pixel of each 2x2 block per frame, for a quarter of the cost\. A still view looks the same as at full rate\.<br>3: Sixteenth, one pixel of each 4x4 block per frame, for a sixteenth of the cost\. Noisy and soft in motion\.<br>The quarter and sixteenth rates need Cloud Temporal History Weight above 0\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudMultiScatterOctaves|int|3|||How many orders of light scattering inside the clouds are approximated, from 1 to 4\. 3 is a good balance\. 1 keeps only light scattered once, which makes cloud interiors dark\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudNoiseTileKm|float|12|||How far, in kilometres, the cloud detail pattern runs before it repeats\. Smaller values show repetition more\. Larger values give coarser detail\. 6 to 24 works well\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudPerformancePreset|int|0|0|1|A switch for cloud shortcuts that are faster but look slightly different\.<br>0: Quality\.<br>1: Performance\. Each cloud sample takes its sun shadow from a simpler shadow map\. Fine billow detail is softer, and billow tops look slightly brighter when the sun is low\.<br>The cloud march rate is set separately by Cloud March Rate\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudPhaseG1|float|0.8|||How strongly clouds scatter light forward\. It creates the bright silver lining at the edges of clouds in front of the sun\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudProfilingLog|bool|False|||Writes cloud GPU timings to the log every 120 frames\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudProfilingMode|int|0|||A cloud diagnostic mode\. 0: Normal\. 9: Reference, a slow, exact path tracer for the cloud lighting, for comparing against the normal mode\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudQuarterMarch|bool|True|||Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.cloudMarchRate \(2 = Quarter, 1 = Full, 0 = Auto\)\. An existing value is migrated automatically\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudRenderRTEnable|bool|True|||Draws the clouds where the camera sees the sky\. Off, the directly visible sky is cloudless and the on\-screen cloud pass is skipped, unless a debug view needs it\. Reflections and indirect light still get their clouds from the reflection dome\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudScale|float|0|||Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.unitsPerMeter and rtx\.atmosphere\.worldScale\. An existing value is migrated automatically\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudSecondaryLutEnable|bool|True|||Gives reflections and indirect light their clouds from a small sky dome rebuilt every frame instead of marching the clouds per ray\. This saves a lot of GPU time on cloudy skies\. Off, reflections and indirect light see a cloudless sky\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudSecondaryLutInterleaveMode|int|2|||How much of the cloud reflection dome is updated each frame\. The rest is at most a few frames old\. The whole dome is still rebuilt when the clouds change noticeably or the camera cuts\.<br>0: All of it every frame\.<br>1: Half\.<br>2: A quarter\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudShadowFactorStrength|float|1|||Deprecated and ignored: cloud shadows come from the clouds themselves\. Use rtx\.atmosphere\.cloudShadowStrength to fade them\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudShadowMapBakeRowsPerFrame|int|32|8|256|Cloud shadow map rebuilds are spread over several frames, this many rows \(out of 256\) per frame\. Lower values make frames cheaper but follow a moving sun or camera more slowly\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudShadowMapEnable|bool|True|||Stores the clouds' shading toward the sun in a shadow map, rebuilt as the sun moves, instead of marching toward the sun for every cloud sample\. It is much faster\. Turn it off only to compare against the slow reference\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudShadowMapMaxLagDegrees|float|0.25|0|10|While the sun moves fast, such as with a sped\-up time cycle, the shadow map is rebuilt faster so it trails the sun by at most this many degrees\. With a slow sun it costs nothing extra\. 0 always uses Bake Rows per Frame\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudShadowMapRebakeDegrees|float|0.1|0|10|How many degrees the sun has to move before the cloud shadow map is rebuilt\. The map is also rebuilt when the camera moves a quarter of its width or the clouds change\. 0 rebuilds it continuously\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudShadowMapWindowKm|float|6|1|50|The half\-width, in kilometres, of the most detailed cloud shadow map level around the camera\. Each further level is about 3\.3 times wider, and the last reaches the horizon\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudShadowMarchStrength|float|1|||Deprecated and ignored: cloud shadows come from the clouds themselves\. Use rtx\.atmosphere\.cloudShadowStrength to fade them\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudShadowStrength|float|1|||How strongly the clouds shade the sunlight on the ground and in the air, from 0 to 1\. 1 is physical and 0 removes cloud shadows\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudSixteenthMarch|bool|False|||Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.cloudMarchRate = 3 \(Sixteenth\)\. An existing value is migrated automatically\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudSkyAmbientCloudOcclusionStrength|float|1|||How much the clouds change the sky light in the air, from 0 to 1: they hide the sky behind them and add their own scattered light\. 1 is physical\. 0 ignores cloud cover and is meant for debugging\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudSpatialDenoise|float|0.1|0|1|An edge\-aware blur on the clouds after the temporal filter, for the noise left where there is little history, such as while flying or inside a cloud\. The value is the brightness difference, as a share, that still gets smoothed: 0\.1 is about 10%\. It changes only the displayed image, not the history\. 0 turns it off\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudSunOctaveA|float|0.45|0|1|How much less each further order of scattered sunlight is dimmed by the cloud\. Lower values let more light through, for brighter, softer cloud interiors\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudSunOctaveB|float|0.55|0|1|How much each further order of scattered sunlight contributes\. Higher values add more scattered light overall\. Keep it at or below Octave A to stay plausible\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudSunOctaveC|float|0.5|0|1|How directional each further order of scattered sunlight is\. Lower values spread the later orders more evenly in all directions\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudSunOctaveCount|int|6|1|8|How many orders of sunlight scattering inside the clouds are approximated, from 1 to 8\. Dense clouds \(high Cloud Density\) need more to stay bright inside\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudTemporalHistoryWeight|float|0.95|0|0.98|How much of the previous frames the cloud filter keeps while the camera or the clouds move\. 0\.95 averages about 20 frames\. Higher values hide more noise but smear more in motion\. 0 turns the filter off and shows the raw, noisy march\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudTemporalStillSamples|int|64|1|1024|How many frames each cloud pixel averages while the view and the clouds are still\. Higher values give calmer still images but take longer to settle after a change the filter does not detect\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudThickness|float|3.05|||Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.cloudDepthMeters\. An existing value is migrated automatically\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudTypeMean|float|0|||The average cloud type across the sky, from 0 to 1: 0 is stratus, 0\.5 stratocumulus and 1 cumulus\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudTypeNoiseScale|float|0.0034|||How large the regions of different cloud type are\. Smaller values give larger regions\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudTypeSpread|float|0.54|||How much the cloud type varies across the sky, from 0 \(the same everywhere\) to 1 \(the full range\)\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudUnitsPerMeter|float|0|0||Deprecated: a second, clouds\-only units\-per\-metre\. Its ratio to rtx\.atmosphere\.unitsPerMeter is migrated into rtx\.atmosphere\.worldScale, which keeps the clouds where they were\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudVerticalOffsetWorldUnits|float|0|||Moves the whole cloud field up or down, in game world units along the up axis\. It changes where the clouds sit without changing their size, the ground level or the haze\. Center Layer at Player sets it once to place the main layer around the player\. Center again after changing World Scale or the layer depth\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudViewSamples|int|32|||The base number of march steps through the cloud layer, from 1 to 32, used while Cloud Step Spacing is 0\. Higher values look better and cost more GPU time\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudViewSamplesMax|int|128|||The maximum number of march steps per ray through the main cloud layer, from 2 to 256\. Rays that leave the clouds or become opaque stop earlier\. Lower it to limit the worst\-case GPU cost\. It has no effect while Cloud Step Spacing is 0\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudViewStepKm|float|0.25|||The target spacing of cloud samples, in kilometres, from 0 to 4\. Larger spacing saves GPU time but can lose detail or miss thin clouds\. Max Cloud Samples separately limits the steps per ray\. 0 uses the fixed step count of Cloud View Samples\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudVoxelGridRebakeGranularityKm|float|0.1|||How far, in kilometres, the clouds or the camera have to move before the clouds count as changed\. A change restarts the gradual updates of the reflection dome and the cloud history\. 0 counts any movement\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudVoxelShadowsEnable|bool|True|||Casts cloud shadows from the cloud shadow map, on the air and on the ground\. Off, there are no cloud shadows on either\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudWindDirection|float|45|||The direction the clouds drift toward, in degrees \(0 = \+X, 90 = \+Z\)\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudWindSpeed|float|0.02|||How fast the clouds drift, in kilometres per second\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.cloudWorldCompression|float|1|0.1||Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.worldScale \(1 divided by this value\)\. An existing value is migrated automatically\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.dayLengthMinutes|float|24|0.01||How many real minutes one full 24\-hour day lasts while the time cycle runs\. 24 gives one minute per in\-game hour\. Raise it for a slower day\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.dayOfYear|int|80|1|365|The day of the year, from 1 to 365, which sets the season\. 80 is the March equinox \(sunrise due east, sunset due west, a 12\-hour day\), 172 the June solstice and 355 the December solstice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.debugDispatchCloudRender|bool|True|||Diagnostic: runs the on\-screen cloud pass\. It is skipped anyway while the clouds or Cloud Render are off and no debug view is shown\. Turn it off to measure the pass's cost\. The clouds then freeze in place\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.debugDispatchCloudSkyTransmittance|bool|True|||Diagnostic: runs the small per\-frame pass that measures how much the clouds hide the sky\. Turn it off to measure its cost\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.debugDispatchSkyLuts|bool|True|||Diagnostic: rebuilds the sky's lookup tables when the sun or the atmosphere changes\. Turn it off to freeze them and measure their cost\. The sky then stops following the sun\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.debugEnableSkyMissShading|bool|True|||Diagnostic: shades rays that reach the sky\. Turn it off to draw a flat grey sky and measure the cost of sky shading\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.directionalLightRadianceScale|float|1|||A multiplier on the brightness of the sun and moon lights the sky adds to the scene\. Adjust it if sunlight and moonlight on surfaces read too bright or too dim overall\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.flipUpAxis|bool|False|||Flips the world's up axis for the sky\. Turn it on when the sky renders upside down, with the bright zenith below you and the dark ground half overhead\.<br>A sky that looks rotated 90 degrees is a different problem: rtx\.zUp does not match the game\. To tell them apart, stop the time cycle and set a mid\-height sun: a zenith off to one side means rtx\.zUp, a zenith under your feet means this setting\.<br>Flipping the axis also reverses the direction the sun travels across the sky\. Sun Rotation and North Offset cannot undo that, so the sun's arc has to be placed again afterwards\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.fogAlbedo|float|0.9|0|1|The share of the height fog's dimming that is scattered light rather than absorbed light\. Lower values give a darker, smokier fog\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.fogAnisotropy|float|0.3|-0.95|0.95|How strongly the height fog scatters light forward, from \-0\.95 to 0\.95\. Positive values brighten the fog toward the sun and around lamps\. Water\-droplet fog scatters strongly forward, smoke much less\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.fogBaseHeightMeters|float|0|||The height fog's base, in metres above the ground level\. The fog has its full visibility here and thins above it\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.fogHeightFalloffMeters|float|100|1||How quickly the height fog thins with height: its density falls by a factor of e every this many metres above the base\. Large values give an even fog\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.fogNearFadeMeters|float|40|0|1000|A clear zone around the camera for ground\-level fog\. The height fog and cloud layer 2 fade in from nothing at the camera to full density at this distance, so the player's surroundings stay readable in thick fog\. It is a readability aid, not physical\. 0 turns it off\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.fogVisibilityMeters|float|0|0||Height fog: the visibility at the fog's base height, in metres, which is the distance at which contrast falls to 2%\. 0 means no fog\. 50 is thick fog, 1000 mist and 10000 light haze\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.groundAlbedo|float|0.3|0|1|The share of light the ground reflects\. Light bounced off the ground brightens the lower sky, lights the ground seen below the horizon and lights cloud bases from below\. Set it to the scene's typical ground: snow lights cloud bases more than forest or sea\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.groundLevelAuto|bool|True|||Measures the ground level from the scene instead of using Ground Level\. Rays cast straight down \(or ground points the game supplies\) build a smoothed map of the ground height around the player, and the ground level is that map under the camera\. Cloud bases, the height fog and layer 2 then keep their heights above the local ground as the player travels, while climbing a building or jumping changes nothing\. Needs ray\-traced scene geometry or ground points from the game\. Without either, Ground Level is used\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.groundLevelWorldUnits|float|0|||The height, in the game's world units along its up axis, that counts as ground level \(altitude zero\) while Ground Level: Auto is off, or before Auto has measured anything\. Cloud heights, the height fog and the sky's altitude are measured from it\. The easiest way to set it is the Set to Here button, used while standing on ground the game treats as sea level\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.groundSmoothingMeters|float|4000|100||The distance, in metres, over which Ground Level: Auto averages the measured ground\. Larger values follow only the broad shape of the land and move the cloud deck less as the player travels\. Smaller values follow hills and valleys more closely\. Changing it starts a fresh measurement\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.hazeWorldScale|float|1|0.001|1000|The size of the haze's world relative to the game's: World Scale for the haze, height fog, sun shafts and local\-light glow alone\. 1 is real size, so the game's terrain is hazed at its true distance\. With World Scale below 1 and this left at 1, the clouds shrink but distant land is not hazed as if it were farther away\. Settings in metres that belong to the haze \(fog distances and heights, the froxel range\) scale with it\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.latitudeDegrees|float|45|-90|90|The observer's latitude in degrees, positive north\. It sets how high the sun climbs and how tilted its path is\. At 0 \(the equator\) the sun passes almost straight overhead\. High latitudes keep it low, with long, shallow sunrises and sunsets\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.lightningColor|float3|0.72, 0.78, 1|||The colour of a lightning flash, in linear RGB, for both the cloud glow and the scene light\. Only the hue counts\. The brightness is set by Lightning Luminous Intensity\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.lightningEnable|bool|True|||Turns lightning on or off\. While on, Lightning Strikes per Minute sets how often it strikes, and the storm weather presets raise that rate\. Turn it off to silence lightning everywhere, including the storm presets and the Test Strike button\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.lightningFlashIntensity|float|50|||Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.lightningLuminousIntensity\. Ignored\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.lightningLuminousIntensity|float|2e+08|0||The peak brightness of a lightning flash, in candela\. It drives the glow in the clouds, the glow in the haze and the light on the scene\. The default is realistic: a flash lights the ground a few kilometres away like a dim overcast day, outshines everything at night, and is barely visible against a daylit cloud\. Raise it for a dramatic daytime storm\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.lightningRangeKm|float|15|||The farthest distance from the camera a strike can land, in kilometres, from 1\.5 to 30\. Strikes spread evenly over the area between 1 km and this distance\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.lightningSceneLight|bool|True|||Adds each flash to the scene as a light at the strike\. Off, the flash lights only the clouds and the air\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.lightningSceneLightIntensity|float|2000|||Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.lightningLuminousIntensity \(its strength\) and rtx\.atmosphere\.lightningSceneLight \(on or off\)\. Ignored\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.lightningStrikesPerMinute|float|0|||The average number of lightning strikes per minute, from 0 to 60\. The gaps between strikes are random, so strikes cluster and pause naturally\. 0 means no automatic strikes\. Test Strike still works\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.mieAbsorption|float3|0.0044, 0.0044, 0.0044|||The haze's base absorption coefficients per colour channel, per kilometre\. Real haze absorbs about as much light as it scatters\. Raising this relative to Mie Scattering darkens the haze, and making it differ between channels tints it: this is how dust becomes brown and dim, or smoke grey and dark, rather than only denser\. Scaled by Aerosol Density\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.mieAnisotropy|float|0.8|||How strongly the haze scatters light forward, from \-1 to 1\. It sets the size and brightness of the glow around the sun\. 0\.8 is typical of Earth's haze\. The sun's disc itself is drawn separately, at Sun Size\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.mieScattering|float3|0.003996, 0.003996, 0.003996|||The haze's base scattering coefficients per colour channel, per kilometre\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon0.angularDiameter|float|0.518|||The moon's size in the sky, in degrees\. 0\.518 is the size of Earth's Moon\. Its total light stays the same at any size, so a bigger disc has a dimmer surface\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon0.angularRadius|float|0.518|||Deprecated: renamed angularDiameter, which takes the same value\. An existing value is migrated\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon0.color|float3|1, 1, 1|||The moon's surface brightness and colour relative to Earth's Moon\. 1, 1, 1 is lunar\. It scales the moon's light and its disc alike\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon0.elevation|float|45|||The moon's height above the horizon, in degrees\. A game integration can set it every frame, which overrides the saved value\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon0.enabled|bool|False|||Draws this extra moon, placed by its elevation and rotation, for a game that drives its own moon\. Turn rtx\.atmosphere\.realMoonEnabled off when it replaces the real moon\. Its phase, light and earthshine follow from its position and the sun's, like the real moon's\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon0.phase|float|-1|-1|1|The moon's phase as a point in its cycle: 0 new, 0\.25 first quarter, 0\.5 full and 0\.75 last quarter\. A negative value takes the phase from the moon's position relative to the sun, like the real moon\. Set it for a game whose moon follows its own phase calendar\. Its light, earthshine and lit side follow this phase\. A game integration can set it every frame\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon0.rotation|float|90|||The moon's compass direction \(azimuth\), in degrees\. A game integration can set it every frame, which overrides the saved value\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon1.angularDiameter|float|0.518|||The moon's size in the sky, in degrees\. 0\.518 is the size of Earth's Moon\. Its total light stays the same at any size, so a bigger disc has a dimmer surface\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon1.angularRadius|float|0.518|||Deprecated: renamed angularDiameter, which takes the same value\. An existing value is migrated\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon1.color|float3|1, 1, 1|||The moon's surface brightness and colour relative to Earth's Moon\. 1, 1, 1 is lunar\. It scales the moon's light and its disc alike\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon1.elevation|float|45|||The moon's height above the horizon, in degrees\. A game integration can set it every frame, which overrides the saved value\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon1.enabled|bool|False|||Draws this extra moon, placed by its elevation and rotation, for a game that drives its own moon\. Turn rtx\.atmosphere\.realMoonEnabled off when it replaces the real moon\. Its phase, light and earthshine follow from its position and the sun's, like the real moon's\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon1.phase|float|-1|-1|1|The moon's phase as a point in its cycle: 0 new, 0\.25 first quarter, 0\.5 full and 0\.75 last quarter\. A negative value takes the phase from the moon's position relative to the sun, like the real moon\. Set it for a game whose moon follows its own phase calendar\. Its light, earthshine and lit side follow this phase\. A game integration can set it every frame\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon1.rotation|float|90|||The moon's compass direction \(azimuth\), in degrees\. A game integration can set it every frame, which overrides the saved value\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon2.angularDiameter|float|0.518|||The moon's size in the sky, in degrees\. 0\.518 is the size of Earth's Moon\. Its total light stays the same at any size, so a bigger disc has a dimmer surface\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon2.angularRadius|float|0.518|||Deprecated: renamed angularDiameter, which takes the same value\. An existing value is migrated\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon2.color|float3|1, 1, 1|||The moon's surface brightness and colour relative to Earth's Moon\. 1, 1, 1 is lunar\. It scales the moon's light and its disc alike\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon2.elevation|float|45|||The moon's height above the horizon, in degrees\. A game integration can set it every frame, which overrides the saved value\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon2.enabled|bool|False|||Draws this extra moon, placed by its elevation and rotation, for a game that drives its own moon\. Turn rtx\.atmosphere\.realMoonEnabled off when it replaces the real moon\. Its phase, light and earthshine follow from its position and the sun's, like the real moon's\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon2.phase|float|-1|-1|1|The moon's phase as a point in its cycle: 0 new, 0\.25 first quarter, 0\.5 full and 0\.75 last quarter\. A negative value takes the phase from the moon's position relative to the sun, like the real moon\. Set it for a game whose moon follows its own phase calendar\. Its light, earthshine and lit side follow this phase\. A game integration can set it every frame\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon2.rotation|float|90|||The moon's compass direction \(azimuth\), in degrees\. A game integration can set it every frame, which overrides the saved value\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon3.angularDiameter|float|0.518|||The moon's size in the sky, in degrees\. 0\.518 is the size of Earth's Moon\. Its total light stays the same at any size, so a bigger disc has a dimmer surface\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon3.angularRadius|float|0.518|||Deprecated: renamed angularDiameter, which takes the same value\. An existing value is migrated\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon3.color|float3|1, 1, 1|||The moon's surface brightness and colour relative to Earth's Moon\. 1, 1, 1 is lunar\. It scales the moon's light and its disc alike\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon3.elevation|float|45|||The moon's height above the horizon, in degrees\. A game integration can set it every frame, which overrides the saved value\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon3.enabled|bool|False|||Draws this extra moon, placed by its elevation and rotation, for a game that drives its own moon\. Turn rtx\.atmosphere\.realMoonEnabled off when it replaces the real moon\. Its phase, light and earthshine follow from its position and the sun's, like the real moon's\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon3.phase|float|-1|-1|1|The moon's phase as a point in its cycle: 0 new, 0\.25 first quarter, 0\.5 full and 0\.75 last quarter\. A negative value takes the phase from the moon's position relative to the sun, like the real moon\. Set it for a game whose moon follows its own phase calendar\. Its light, earthshine and lit side follow this phase\. A game integration can set it every frame\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.moon3.rotation|float|90|||The moon's compass direction \(azimuth\), in degrees\. A game integration can set it every frame, which overrides the saved value\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.multiScatterPhysicalStrength|float|1|||Blends between a simple approximation of light that scatters many times in the air \(0\) and the physical calculation \(1\)\. 1 gives a natural gradient from the zenith to a warm horizon\. Lower values flatten the gradient and turn the horizon bluer\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.multiScatterStrength|float|1|||Deprecated and ignored: the multiple scattering is always physical\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.nightGainStops|float|0|0|16|Night Gain, in stops\. 0 is physical\. Brightens all natural night light together \(the moons, the moonlit sky, starlight, airglow\) so the sky, clouds and ground stay consistent\.<br>Small values, about \+1 to \+3, make up for the eye's dark adaptation, which a display cannot reproduce, so a physical night looks darker on screen than in reality\. Large values, up to \+10 and beyond, balance the night against a game whose own lights are much brighter than physical\.<br>The sun, twilight and lightning are not changed, and the gain fades in through twilight so the sky never brightens after sunset\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.nightSkyStars|bool|True|||Draws the real night sky: about 25,700 catalogue stars as points and the fainter stars as the Milky Way, at their real brightness and colour\. The stars turn with the date, time and latitude, dim and redden near the horizon, twinkle, and light the clouds and the ground\. Needs the night sky data files\. Turn it off for a game that draws its own stars\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.northOffsetDegrees|float|0|-360|360|Rotates the sun's whole path so that north in the sky lines up with north in the game world\. Adjust it until the sun rises in the direction the game treats as east\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.nubis3AdaptiveStepKm|float|0.025|||The smallest cloud march step, in kilometres, from 0 to 0\.2\. Steps start this fine near the camera to resolve close detail and grow with distance\. 0 uses an even step length everywhere\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.nubis3ErosionStrength|float|0.58|||How strongly the detail noise carves into the clouds, from 0 to 2\. 0 gives smooth blobs, 1 the standard look and higher values ragged, heavily carved clouds\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.nubis3JitterAnimateKm|float|1e+09|0||The distance, in kilometres, beyond which the cloud sampling noise stops changing every frame\. By default it never stops\. Lower values make distant cloud noise hold still on screen instead of shimmering\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.nvdfCoverageOffsetKm|float|0.2|||How much the clouds grow or shrink, in kilometres, per unit of coverage change from the coverage the shape field was built for, from 0 to 4\. Higher values make coverage changes grow clouds faster, so they merge sooner at high coverage\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.nvdfNominalCoverage|float|0|||The cloud coverage the cloud shape field is built for, from 0 to 1\. 0 \(Auto\) follows the current coverage and rebuilds the field in the background when it changes\. A fixed value is meant for debugging and look tuning\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.nvdfProfileDepthKm|float|0.12|||How far into a cloud, in kilometres, its density takes to build up from the edge, from 0\.1 to 3\. Small values give dense, hard\-edged clouds\. Large values give soft, translucent edges\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.nvdfStepScale|float|0.95|||How far the cloud march jumps through empty air, as a share of the distance to the nearest cloud, from 0 to 0\.95\. Higher values save GPU time, especially toward the horizon\. 0 steps evenly\. Lower it if cloud outlines show onion\-like banding\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.ozoneAbsorption|float3|0.00065, 0.001881, 8.5e-05|||The ozone layer's base absorption coefficients per colour channel, per kilometre\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.ozoneDensity|float|1|||A multiplier on the density of the ozone layer\. 1 is typical\. Ozone gives the twilight sky its blue colour\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.ozoneLayerAltitude|float|25|||The height of the ozone layer's densest point, in kilometres\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.ozoneLayerWidth|float|15|||The half\-thickness of the ozone layer, in kilometres\. Together with Ozone Density it sets the total amount of ozone\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.planetRadius|float|6371|||The planet's radius, in kilometres\. 6371 is Earth\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.qualityLevel|int|0|0|2|Scales the GPU cost of the clouds and the haze for slower GPUs\. It works on top of the other quality settings and changes none of them\.<br>0: High, the full quality\.<br>1: Medium\. The clouds march a quarter of their pixels each frame at every resolution, and the near\-field haze uses larger froxels and fewer depth slices\. A still view looks nearly the same; in motion the clouds are slightly softer\.<br>2: Low\. Medium's savings, plus the faster cloud sun shadows of Cloud Performance Preset 1, fewer cloud samples per ray and a coarser haze grid\. Fine billow detail and sun shafts are softer\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.radianceAutoScale|bool|True|||Scales everything the sky outputs by a power of two that follows the light level, so a night sky, which is about a hundred\-millionth as bright as the day sky, keeps its precision in 16\-bit render targets\. Daylight always uses a scale of 1\. Turn it off only for debugging: a moonless night then turns black in 16\-bit targets\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.rayleighScattering|float3|0.005802, 0.013558, 0.0331|||The air's base scattering coefficients per colour channel, per kilometre\. They give the sky its blue colour\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.realMoonAngularDiameter|float|0.518|0.05|20|The drawn size of the real moon, in degrees\. 0\.518 is the Moon's real size\. A larger disc gives the same total moonlight, so its surface looks dimmer\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.realMoonEnabled|bool|True|||Draws Earth's Moon where it really is for the configured date, time and latitude\. Its phase, brightness, earthshine and eclipses follow from where the sun is\. It lights the sky, the clouds and the ground\. Turn it off for a game that places its own moon with rtx\.atmosphere\.moon0\-3\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.seaLevelWorldKm|float|0|||Deprecated: replaced by rtx\.atmosphere\.groundLevelWorldUnits\. An existing value is converted automatically\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.skyIndirectRadianceScale|float|1|0||A multiplier on the sky light that reaches surfaces through diffuse bounces\. 1 is physical\. Raise it when shaded areas look too dull next to sunlit ones\. Reflections and the visible sky keep their physical brightness, so reflections still match the sky\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.skyLutCacheKeySplitEnable|bool|True|||Recalculates each part of the sky only when its own inputs change, so a moving sun updates only the small sky view table\. The image is unchanged\. Turn it off only to compare against recalculating everything together\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.skyViewAltitudeRebakeGranularityKm|float|0.05|||How far, in kilometres, the camera has to climb or descend before the sky is recalculated for the new height\. The default is 0\.05 \(50 m\)\. 0 never recalculates for height, so the sky stays as seen from where it was last calculated\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.skyViewRebakeGranularityDeg|float|0.1|||How many degrees the sun or a moon has to move before the sky is recalculated\. 0\.1 follows the sun smoothly at a small cost\. 0 recalculates every frame while the sun moves\. Other sky changes always recalculate at once\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.starScintillation|float|1|0|4|How strongly the stars twinkle, relative to a typical night \(1\)\. Stars near the horizon twinkle most and can flash colours, while high stars stay steady\. 0 turns twinkling off\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.sunElevation|float|15|||The sun's height above the horizon, in degrees\. A game integration can set it every frame, which overrides the saved value\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.sunIlluminance|float3|15, 15, 15|||The sun's base colour and brightness\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.sunIntensity|float|1.09|||The strength of the sun\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.sunRotation|float|0|||The sun's compass direction \(azimuth\), in degrees\. A game integration can set it every frame, which overrides the saved value\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.sunShadowSoftnessDeg|float|0|||Sun shadow softness, as the sun light's angular radius in degrees\. 0 is physical and follows Sun Size\. A value above 0 softens or sharpens shadows without changing the visible sun disc, for soft shadows under a small sun\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.sunSize|float|0.545|||The angular diameter of the sun's disc, in degrees\. 0\.545 is close to the real sun\. The sun's shadow softness follows it unless Sun Shadow Softness is set\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.sunsetSaturation|float|1|||Deprecated and ignored: colour grading belongs in the tonemapper\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.timeCycleEnable|bool|False|||Moves the sun with a built\-in clock instead of Sun Elevation and Sun Rotation\. Use it for games without a day and night cycle of their own\. While on, the clock controls the sun, and those two settings \(and any values a game sends for them\) are ignored\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.timeOfDayHours|float|12|0|24|The time of day in hours, from 0 to 24\. 12 is solar noon\. Around an equinox, 6 is about sunrise and 18 about sunset\. While the time cycle runs, this is the start time, and editing it moves the clock\. While the cycle is off it still places the sun, so it works as a manual time\-of\-day control\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.unitsPerMeter|float|0|0||How many game units make one real metre\. This measures the game and is not a look setting: for example 100 for an engine that works in centimetres, or 39\.37 for one that works in inches\. 0 takes the value from rtx\.sceneScale\. The clouds, sky, haze, fog, local\-light glow and lightning all convert with it\. To make the sky smaller than the game's real scale, change World Scale and leave this at the true value\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.useCameraWorldOverride|bool|False|||Anchors the clouds to rtx\.atmosphere\.cameraWorldOverride instead of the camera position the renderer sees\. Turn it on for games that render relative to the camera, where the camera always appears to sit at the origin and the clouds would otherwise move with the view\. The game integration then has to send the real camera position every frame\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.viewAltitudeKm|float|0|||Deprecated: folded into rtx\.atmosphere\.groundLevelWorldUnits\. An existing value is converted automatically\. Save the config again to stop the notice\.|
|rtx.atmosphere.worldScale|float|1|0.001|1000|The size of the sky's world relative to the game's\. The planet, atmosphere, clouds, wind and lightning are all scaled by it together, so a scaled sky looks like the full\-size one, only smaller\. The haze, fog and local\-light glow follow Haze World Scale instead\. 1 is real size\. Lower it for a map built much smaller than the place it depicts, where real\-size clouds look too high and too large \(0\.1 makes the sky a tenth of the size\)\.<br>Settings given in metres or kilometres \(cloud heights and depths, the lightning range\) scale with it\. Settings in world units \(Ground Level, Cloud Vertical Offset, Camera World Override\) are the game's own units and do not\.|
|rtx.autoExposure.enabled|bool|True|||Adjusts exposure automatically, so the image is neither too bright nor too dark\. The meter is the incident\-light meter of the Halo CE path tracer addon by halocedlaart \(rtx\.autoExposure\.halo\.\*\): a center\-weighted mean of each pixel's lighting channel average, with albedo divided out and the view model excluded, limited in EV and adapted at the same speed at any frame rate\. rtx\.tonemap\.exposureBias adds an EV offset on top\.|
|rtx.autoExposure.halo.addonFaithful|bool|False|||Halo Meter: uses the addon's exact sky handling: the sky metered at albedo 1 with full weight at any brightness, no mean floor and no hold\. Overrides rtx\.autoExposure\.halo\.skyWeightMax, skyLumLow, skyLumHigh, meanFloorEv and minCoverage\. Use it with rtx\.autoExposure\.halo\.maxEv = 8 for the addon's limit\.|
|rtx.autoExposure.halo.centreWeight|float|0.75|||Halo Meter: how much more the middle of the frame counts than the edges\. 0 meters the whole frame evenly, 1 ignores the edges entirely\. The falloff is a circle in units of half the frame height, centered 10% of the frame height below the middle so the sky at the top counts less\.|
|rtx.autoExposure.halo.compensationDayEv|float|12|||Halo Meter compensation curve: the scene EV100 at and above which no compensation applies\. An interior is about 5 to 7, an overcast day 12 and sunlight 15\. With the night at EV 0 and the day at 12, a dim interior gets about half the night bias, so it stays darker than mid grey\.|
|rtx.autoExposure.halo.compensationNightEv|float|0|||Halo Meter compensation curve: the scene EV100 at and below which the full rtx\.autoExposure\.halo\.compensationNightStops applies\. A moonlit landscape is about \-2 to \-3, deep twilight or a street under lamps about 0 to 4\. EV100 needs a calibrated sky provider \(NUMOS\)\. Without one it is measured in Remix units\.|
|rtx.autoExposure.halo.compensationNightStops|float|0|||Halo Meter compensation curve: the exposure bias, in stops, for scenes at or below rtx\.autoExposure\.halo\.compensationNightEv \(the EV100 of the metered grey card\)\. It fades linearly to 0 at rtx\.autoExposure\.halo\.compensationDayEv\. Negative values keep nights darker than mid grey, as they look to the eye \(about \-2 to \-4\)\. 0 = no curve\.|
|rtx.autoExposure.halo.demodulateAlbedo|bool|True|||Halo Meter: meters the lighting rather than the image\. Each pixel is divided by its albedo \(at least 0\.05\) and multiplied by 0\.18, so dark and pale textures under the same light meter the same\. Sky pixels count as albedo 1\. This is the addon's behaviour\. Turn it off only to compare\.|
|rtx.autoExposure.halo.excludeViewModel|bool|True|||Halo Meter: ignores view model pixels \(the first\-person weapon and hands\), like the addon's first\-person meter mask\.|
|rtx.autoExposure.halo.maxEv|float|4|||Halo Meter: the highest exposure, in stops\. 4 \(16x\) suits a game in its own light units and keeps dark interiors and nights from being lifted into noise\. The addon uses 8 \(256x\)\. A game calibrated to physical units through a sky provider \(NUMOS, where scene EV100 is about meter EV \+ 16\) needs about 16, and keeps nights and interiors dark with the compensation curve \(rtx\.autoExposure\.halo\.compensation\*\)\. Range 0 to 24\.|
|rtx.autoExposure.halo.meanFloorEv|float|-24|||Halo Meter: the darkest scene the meter believes, in stops \(log2 of the metered grey\-card level\)\. Exposure can then never exceed target / 2^meanFloorEv, however dark the frame, which acts as a second exposure limit in scene terms\. \-24 = off, so rtx\.autoExposure\.halo\.maxEv is the only limit\. With a physically calibrated sky \(NUMOS\), values above about \-20 hold every night at a fixed exposure\. In a game's own units it only matters if set above log2\(target\) \- maxEv\.|
|rtx.autoExposure.halo.minCoverage|float|0.05|||Halo Meter: holds the current exposure while less than this fraction of the centre\-weighted frame is scene surface \(not sky or view model\), for example when looking straight up at a night sky\. 0 = never hold\. Range 0 to 0\.5\.|
|rtx.autoExposure.halo.minEv|float|-16|||Halo Meter: the lowest exposure, in stops\. The addon allows \-24 to 0\.|
|rtx.autoExposure.halo.skyLumHigh|float|0.1|||Halo Meter: sky radiance at or above which a sky pixel has the full rtx\.autoExposure\.halo\.skyWeightMax weight\. Set it at or below skyLumLow for full weight at any brightness\.|
|rtx.autoExposure.halo.skyLumLow|float|0.01|||Halo Meter: sky radiance \(the linear channel average, before exposure\) at or below which a sky pixel has no metering weight\.|
|rtx.autoExposure.halo.skyWeightMax|float|1|||Halo Meter: the metering weight of a bright sky pixel relative to scene surfaces\. The weight fades with the sky's own brightness between rtx\.autoExposure\.halo\.skyLumLow and skyLumHigh, so a day sky counts, dusk less and a night sky barely at all\. Otherwise a night sky meters as near\-black and pushes exposure to its maximum\. 0 = never meter the sky\.|
|rtx.autoExposure.halo.speed|float|2|||Halo Meter: adaptation speed\. Each frame moves exposure 1 \- exp\(\-dt \* speed\) of the way to its target, with dt the frame time in seconds \(at most 0\.25\), so it adapts the same at any frame rate\. The addon allows 0\.1 to 16\.|
|rtx.autoExposure.halo.target|float|0.2|||Halo Meter: the brightness the metered frame is driven to, as how bright an 18% grey card in this light comes out\. 0\.18 is a photographic grey card\. Lower values give a darker, more contrasted image\. The addon allows 0\.02 to 0\.6\.|
|rtx.autoUnblockOptionEdits|bool|False|||If true, editing an RtxOption in the Remix UI that is overridden by a stronger config layer clears the stronger value immediately instead of showing a confirmation dialog\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_IMGUI\_AUTO\_UNBLOCK\_OPTION\_EDITS|
|rtx.automation.disableBlockingDialogBoxes|bool|False|||Disables various blocking blocking dialog boxes \(such as popup windows\) requiring user interaction when set to true, otherwise uses default behavior when set to false\.<br>This option is typically meant for automation\-driven execution of Remix where such dialog boxes if present may cause the application to hang due to blocking waiting for user input\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_AUTOMATION\_DISABLE\_BLOCKING\_DIALOG\_BOXES|
|rtx.automation.disableDisplayMemoryStatistics|bool|False|||Disables display of memory statistics in the Remix window\.<br>This option is typically meant for automation of tests for which we don't want non\-deterministic runtime memory statistics to be shown in GUI that is included as part of test image output\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_AUTOMATION\_DISABLE\_DISPLAY\_MEMORY\_STATISTICS|
|rtx.automation.disableUpdateUpscaleFromDlssPreset|bool|False|||Disables updating upscaler from DLSS preset\.<br>This option is typically meant for automation of tests for which we don't want upscaler to be updated based on a DLSS preset\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_AUTOMATION\_DISABLE\_UPDATE\_UPSCALER\_FROM\_DLSS\_PRESET|
|rtx.automation.enableTestTrace|bool|False|||Enables opt\-in frame trace artifacts for automation\-driven image tests\.<br>When enabled, Remix records a bounded frame window around the configured screenshot frame, writes frame\_trace\.jsonl, and appends dxvk\_trace\_\* summary fields to metrics\.txt\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_TEST\_TRACE|
|rtx.automation.suppressAssetLoadingErrors|bool|False|||Suppresses asset loading errors by turning them into warnings\.<br>This option is typically meant for automation of tests for which acceptable asset loading issues are known\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_AUTOMATION\_SUPPRESS\_ASSET\_LOADING\_ERRORS|
|rtx.batchBlasInputBarriers|bool|True|||Reduces CPU cost: records the memory barriers for BLAS build inputs once per frame, before the builds, and only for BLASes that are built or refit, instead of once per BLAS\. The synchronisation is the same\.|
