|rtx.blockInputToGameInUI|bool|True|||If true, input will not be passed to the game when the UI is active\.<br>Can be toggled with Alt \+ Backspace|
|rtx.bloom.burnIntensity|float|1|||Amount of bloom to add to the final image\.|
|rtx.bloom.enable|bool|True|||Enable bloom \- glowing halos around intense, bright areas\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_BLOOM\_ENABLE|
|rtx.bloom.luminanceThreshold|float|0.25|||Adjust the bloom threshold to suppress blooming of the dim areas\. Pixels with luminance lower than the threshold are multiplied by the weight value that smoothly transitions from 1\.0 \(at luminance=threshold\) to 0\.0 \(at luminance=0\)\.|
|rtx.bloom.steps|int|5|1|8|Number of downsampling steps to perform \[1\.\.8\]\. A higher value produces a wider blooming radius\.|
|rtx.calculateLightIntensityUsingLeastSquares|bool|True|||Enable usage of least squares for approximating a light's falloff curve rather than a more basic single point approach\. This will generally result in more accurate matching of the original application's custom light attenuation curves, especially with non physically based linear\-style attenuation\.|
|rtx.camera.correctProjectionYFlip|bool|False|||Some games \(typically Unity titles\) use a projection matrix with a negative Y scale, causing the ray\-traced scene to render upside down\. Enable this to automatically detect and correct the Y\-flip in the projection matrix at runtime\.|
|rtx.camera.enableFreeCamera|bool|False|||Enables free camera\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_ENABLE\_FREE\_CAMERA|
|rtx.camera.freeCameraPitch|float|0|||Free camera's pitch\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_FREE\_CAMERA\_PITCH|
|rtx.camera.freeCameraPosition|float3|0, 0, 0|||Free camera's position\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_FREE\_CAMERA\_POSITION|
|rtx.camera.freeCameraViewRelative|bool|True|||Free camera transform is relative to the view\.|
|rtx.camera.freeCameraYaw|float|0|||Free camera's position\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_FREE\_CAMERA\_YAW|
|rtx.camera.lockFreeCamera|bool|False|||Locks free camera\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_LOCK\_FREE\_CAMERA|
|rtx.camera.useFreeCameraForComponents|bool|True|||Use free camera for graph components when free camera is enabled\.|
|rtx.cameraAnimationAmplitude|float|2|||Amplitude of the free camera's animation\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_FREE\_CAMERA\_ANIMATION\_AMPLITUDE|
|rtx.cameraAnimationMode|int|3|||Free camera's animation mode\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_FREE\_CAMERA\_ANIMATION\_MODE|
|rtx.cameraJitterSequenceLength|int|64|||Sets a camera jitter sequence length \[number of frames\]\. It will loop around once the length is reached\.|
|rtx.cameraSequence.autoLoad|bool|False|||Load camera sequence automatically\.<br>Environment variable: DXVK\_CAMERA\_SEQUENCE\_AUTO\_LOAD|
|rtx.cameraSequence.mode|int|0|||Current mode\.<br>Environment variable: DXVK\_CAMERA\_SEQUENCE\_MODE|
|rtx.cameraShakePeriod|int|20|||Period of the free camera's animation\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_FREE\_CAMERA\_ANIMATION\_PERIOD|
|rtx.capture.correctBakedTransforms|bool|False|||Some games bake world transforms into mesh vertices\. If individually captured<br>meshes appear to be way off in the middle of nowhere OR instanced meshes appear<br>to all have identity xform matrices, enabling will attempt to correct this and<br>improve stage \+ mesh viewability in tools\.<br>Hashes are unaffected\.|
|rtx.capture.maxHostInstancesPerDraw|int|512|||A draw that the host GPU\-instances through the Remix API \(batched statics, grass\) is captured as one instance prim per placement, so modders can see where its replacements will appear\. This limits the prims per such draw, keeping the placements nearest the camera\. 0 captures every placement\. Replacements apply to all placements either way\.|
|rtx.capture.maxInstanceDistance|float|0|||Captures only instances whose bounds come within this distance of the camera, in world units\. 0 captures everything\. Large open worlds submit thousands of distant instances \(LODs and far cells\) that make captures slow and heavy to open\. A skipped instance is simply absent from the capture, and hashes are unaffected\.|
|rtx.capture.metersPerUnit|float|0|||The size of one world unit in metres, written as the capture stage's metersPerUnit so that tools such as the RTX Remix Toolkit show the scene at its real size\. Set it when rtx\.sceneScale is tuned for the look rather than set to the true size of a world unit\. 0 writes rtx\.sceneScale\.|
|rtx.captureDebugImage|bool|False||||
|rtx.captureEnableMultiframe|bool|False|||Enables multi\-frame capturing\. THIS HAS NOT BEEN MAINTAINED AND SHOULD BE USED WITH EXTREME CAUTION\.|
|rtx.captureFramesPerSecond|int|24|||Playback rate marked in the USD stage\.<br>Will eventually determine frequency with which game state is captured and written\. Currently every frame \-\- even those at higher frame rates \-\- are recorded\.|
|rtx.captureInstances|bool|True|||If true, an instanced snapshot of the game scene will be captured and exported to a USD stage, in addition to all meshes, textures, materials, etc\.<br>If false, only meshes, etc will be captured\.|
|rtx.captureMaxFrames|int|1|||Max frames capturable when running a multi\-frame capture\. The capture can be toggled to completion manually\.|
|rtx.captureMeshBlendWeightDelta|float|0.01|||Inter\-frame blend weight min delta warrants new time sample\.|
|rtx.captureMeshColorDelta|float|0.3|||Inter\-frame color min delta warrants new time sample\.|
|rtx.captureMeshNormalDelta|float|0.3|||Inter\-frame normal min delta warrants new time sample\.|
|rtx.captureMeshPositionDelta|float|0.3|||Inter\-frame position min delta warrants new time sample\.|
|rtx.captureMeshTexcoordDelta|float|0.3|||Inter\-frame texcoord min delta warrants new time sample\.|
|rtx.captureNoInstance|bool|False|||Same as 'rtx\.captureInstances' except inverse\. This is the original/old variant, and will be deprecated, however is still functional\.|
|rtx.captureOverwriteExistingCapture|bool|False|||If true, a capture with the same filename will overwrite any existing capture file instead of appending a numeric suffix to avoid collisions\.|
|rtx.captureShowMenuOnHotkey|bool|True|||If true, then the capture menu will appear whenever one of the capture hotkeys are pressed\. A capture MUST be started by using a button in the menu, in that case\.<br>If false, the hotkeys behave as expected\. The user must manually open the menu in order to change any values\.|
|rtx.clearSceneOnLastExternalMeshDestroyed|bool|True|||When the last mesh created through the Remix API is destroyed, immediately clear the scene and release unused DXVK memory chunks, instead of waiting for frames that an API client \(e\.g\. HdRemix\) may never submit once its scene is empty\. Only applies to editor\-mode \(API\-driven\) sessions\. Set to false to restore the purely frame\-driven behavior\.|
|rtx.compactStableBlas|bool|False|||Compacts BLASes that have not been built or refit for rtx\.compactStableBlasFrames frames \(per\-geometry BLASes, and merged buckets without opacity micromaps\)\. Typically saves 30\-50% of BLAS memory\. A compacted BLAS that changes later is replaced by a full\-size one\. The \[AccelMem\] log counts compactions and the memory saved\. The traced result is the same\.|
|rtx.compactStableBlasFrames|int|120|||How many frames a BLAS must go without a build or refit before rtx\.compactStableBlas compacts it\.|
|rtx.compositePrimaryDirectDiffuse|bool|True|||Enables direct lightning's diffuse signal for primary surfaces in the final composite\.|
|rtx.compositePrimaryDirectSpecular|bool|True|||Enables direct lightning's specular signal for primary surfaces in the final composite\.|
|rtx.compositePrimaryIndirectDiffuse|bool|True|||Enables indirect lightning's diffuse signal for primary surfaces in the final composite\.|
|rtx.compositePrimaryIndirectSpecular|bool|True|||Enables indirect lightning's specular signal for primary surfaces in the final composite\.|
|rtx.compositeSecondaryCombinedDiffuse|bool|True|||Enables combined direct and indirect lightning's diffuse signal for secondary surfaces in the final composite\.|
|rtx.compositeSecondaryCombinedSpecular|bool|True|||Enables combined direct and indirect lightning's specular signal for secondary surfaces in the final composite\.|
|rtx.coreMaterial.census|bool|False|||Diagnostic: every 300 frames, logs \[MaterialCensus\] lines describing the scene's surfaces: material types, alpha states, material features and fixed\-function state\. CPU only\. Does not change rendering\.|
|rtx.coreMaterial.gpuCheck|bool|False|||Diagnostic for the compact material records: every 300 frames, a compute pass decodes both material records on the GPU and counts the surfaces that differ\. Logs \[CoreMaterial\] gpuCheck lines two frames later\.|
|rtx.coreMaterial.selfCheck|bool|False|||Diagnostic for the compact material records: every 300 frames, decodes every compact material record on the CPU and compares it with the existing record\. Logs \[CoreMaterial\] check lines with the number of mismatches\.|
|rtx.d3d9v2.earlyChangeTestSelfCheckFrames|int|0|||Diagnostic for the D3D9 frontend's early change test, which skips the preparation of unchanged draws: every this many frames, the draws it would skip are prepared in full and compared with what was stored\. Differences are logged as \[D3D9v2\] preKeyMismatch\. 0 = off\.|
|rtx.d3d9v2.log|bool|False|||D3D9 frontend diagnostic: describes every D3D9 draw sent to ray tracing by its resources and their versions, and every 300 frames logs how many draws repeat last frame's exactly and what the scene did with them\. Counters only\.|
|rtx.d3d9v2.perDrawRenderState|int|0|||Compatibility hook for hosts that keep some draws out of the D3D9 frontend's scene: the D3D9 render state \(1\-255\) whose bit rtx\.d3d9v2\.perDrawRenderStateBit marks a draw as per\-draw\. A marked draw is ray traced only in the frames it is drawn in, with its own fog and its own projection\. With rtx\.viewModel\.enable, a field of view that differs from the main camera's makes it a view\-model draw\. It does not replace a camera that earlier draws of the frame set up, and it never makes the scene rebuild other objects\. Unmarked draws are unaffected\. 0 = off\.|
|rtx.d3d9v2.perDrawRenderStateBit|int|0|||The bit \(0\-31\) of rtx\.d3d9v2\.perDrawRenderState that marks a per\-draw draw\.|
|rtx.d3d9v2.perDrawRenderStateUnmarkedValue|int|0|||A value of rtx\.d3d9v2\.perDrawRenderState that never marks a draw, even when the marker bit is set in it: the value a host writes when it clears that render state \(for example 0xFEFEFEFE\)\. 0 = none\.|
|rtx.debug.logPipelineStats|bool|False|||Logs the driver's shader statistics \(register count, spills, instruction count, binary size and whatever else the driver reports\) for every compute, ray tracing and graphics pipeline Remix creates, as one \[PipelineStats\] line per pipeline in remix\-dxvk\.log, with the Remix shader names\. Takes effect after a restart\. Needs VK\_KHR\_pipeline\_executable\_properties, and can make shader compilation slightly slower\.|
|rtx.debug.stress.delayChance|float|0.1|0|1|Stress mode: the chance, from 0 to 1, that a site visit is delayed while rtx\.debug\.stress\.delayUs is above 0\.|
|rtx.debug.stress.delayUs|int|0|||Stress mode: the longest random delay, in microseconds, at the start of worker thread pool tasks, command stream chunks and scene command drains, so the render thread lags its caller by varying amounts\. 0 turns the delays off\. For finding timing bugs; it slows everything down\.|
|rtx.debug.stress.forceSlotReuse|bool|False|||Stress mode: worker thread pools use one task slot per worker and the staged bone matrix blocks are rewound at every draw, so a slot is reused as early as the code allows\.|
|rtx.debug.stress.seed|int|1|||Stress mode: the seed of the random delays, logged when stress mode starts\. The same seed delays the same visits of each site by the same amounts\. 0 picks a seed from the clock\.|
|rtx.debugView.accumulation.blendMode|int|0|||The blend mode to use for accumulating debug view output\.<br>Supported modes are: 0 = Average, 1 = Min, 2 = Max\.<br>Average is the default mode and is the most common mode to use for accumulation\.<br>Min and Max are useful for visualizing the minimum or maximum value of a debug view output over time\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_DEBUG\_VIEW\_ACCUMULATION\_BLEND\_MODE|
|rtx.debugView.accumulation.enable|bool|False|||Enables accumulation of debug ouptput's result to emulate multiple samples per pixel or over time\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_DEBUG\_VIEW\_ACCUMULATION\_ENABLE|
|rtx.debugView.accumulation.numberOfFramesToAccumulate|int|1024|1||Number of frames to accumulate debug view's result over\.<br>This can be used for generating reference images smoothed over time\.<br>By default the accumulation stops once the limit is reached\.<br>When desired, continous accumulation can be enabled via enableContinuousAccumulation\.|
|rtx.debugView.accumulation.resetOnCameraTransformChange|bool|True|||Resets the accumulated debug view output when the camera transform changes\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_DEBUG\_VIEW\_ACCUMULATION\_RESET\_ON\_CAMERA\_TRANFORM\_CHANGE|
|rtx.debugView.composite.compositeViewIdx|int|0|||Index of a composite view to show when Composite Debug View is enabled\. The index must be a a valid value from CompositeDebugView enumeration\. Value of 0 disables Composite Debug View\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_DEBUG\_VIEW\_COMPOSITE\_VIEW\_INDEX|
|rtx.debugView.composite.numColumnsInRuntimeValuesSets|int|4|||Number of columns in the runtime values sets\. This is used to determine how many values are displayed in the composite view\.|
|rtx.debugView.debugViewIdx|int|0|||Index of a debug view to show when Debug View is enabled\. The index must be a valid value from DEBUG\_VIEW\_\* macro defined indices\. Value of 0 disables Debug View\.<br>Environment variable: DXVK\_RTX\_DEBUG\_VIEW\_INDEX|
|rtx.debugView.displayType|int|0||3|The display type to use for visualizing debug view input values\.<br>Supported display types are: 0 = Standard, 1 = BGR Exclusive Color, 2 = EV100, 3 = HDR Waveform<br>Each mode may be useful for a different kind of visualization, though Standard is typically the most common mode to use\.<br>Standard mode works for a simple direct, scaled or color mapped visualization, BGR exclusive for another type of color mapped visualization, and EV100 or the HDR Waveform for understanding HDR value magnitudes in the input on a log scale\.<br>Environment variable: DXVK\_RTX\_DEBUG\_VIEW\_DISPLAY\_TYPE|
|rtx.debugView.enableGammaCorrection|bool|False|||Enables gamma correction of a debug view value\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_DEBUG\_VIEW\_ENABLE\_GAMMA\_CORRECTION|
|rtx.debugView.enableInputQuantization|bool|False|||Enables uniform\-step input quantization on debug view input buffers\.<br>This is mostly useful for when debugging artifacts relating to quantization that may not be visible in a buffer due to higher precision formats in use\.<br>For example, the final output from tonemapping is a floating point texture in the debug view but will be quantized to 8 bit on some monitors\. Using this option the quantization which will be applied to the output can be visualized in advance\.|
|rtx.debugView.evMaxValue|int|4|||The maximum EV100 debug view input value to map to the top of the visualization range when EV100 debug display is in use\.<br>Values above this value in the input will be clamped to the top of the range\.<br>Environment variable: DXVK\_RTX\_DEBUG\_VIEW\_EV\_MAX\_VALUE|
|rtx.debugView.evMinValue|int|-4|||The minimum EV100 debug view input value to map to the bottom of the visualization range when EV100 debug display is in use\.<br>Values below this value in the input will be clamped to the bottom of the range\.<br>Environment variable: DXVK\_RTX\_DEBUG\_VIEW\_EV\_MIN\_VALUE|
|rtx.debugView.gpuPrint.enable|bool|False|||Enables writing into a GPU buffer that's read by CPU when CTRL is pressed\. The value is printed to console\.|
|rtx.debugView.gpuPrint.pixelIndex|int2|2147483647, 2147483647|||Pixel position to GPU print for\. Requires useMousePosition to be turned off\.|
|rtx.debugView.gpuPrint.useMousePosition|bool|True|||Uses mouse position to select a pixel to GPU print for\.|
|rtx.debugView.inverseQuantizationStepSize|float|255|||The inverse of the uniform step size to quantize the debug view input to when Input Quantization is enabled\.<br>A value of 255 indicates that the input will be quantized to steps of 1/255, the same as the step size used when quantizing the range 0\-1 to an 8 bit representation\.|
|rtx.debugView.maxValue|float|1|||The maximum debug view input value to map to 1 in the output when the standard debug display is in use\. Values above this value in the input will be clamped to 1 in the output\.<br>Environment variable: DXVK\_RTX\_DEBUG\_VIEW\_MAX\_VALUE|
|rtx.debugView.minValue|float|0|||The minimum debug view input value to map to 0 in the output when the standard debug display is in use\. Values below this value in the input will be clamped to 0 in the output\.<br>Environment variable: DXVK\_RTX\_DEBUG\_VIEW\_MIN\_VALUE|
|rtx.debugView.overlayOnTopOfRenderOutput|bool|False|||Enables the debug view to be overlayed directly on top of the rendered output\.<br>Overlays all non zero RGB values from the debug view on top of the rendered output\.<br>Use rtx\.debugView\.overlayOpacity to control the transparency of the overlay\.<br><br>Environment variable: RTX\_DEBUG\_VIEW\_OVERLAY\_ON\_TOP\_OF\_RENDER\_OUTPUT|
|rtx.debugView.overlayOpacity|float|1|||Opacity of the debug view overlay when rtx\.debugView\.overlayOnTopOfRenderOutput is enabled\.<br>  0 == fully transparent, 1 == fully opaque\.<br>|
|rtx.debugView.pseudoColorMode|int|0|||Selects a mode for mapping debug value inputs to a scalar to be visualized using a colormap spectrum\. Only takes effect when rtx\.debugView\.displayType is set to "Standard"\.<br>Supported modes are: 0 = Disabled, 1 = Luminance, 2 = Red, 3 = Green, 4 = Blue, 5 = Alpha\.<br>Useful for visualizing a value's range with greater precision than a simple monochromatic color spectrum \(due to interpolating across an entire spectrum of color with a roughly uniform progression\)\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_DEBUG\_VIEW\_PSEUDO\_COLOR\_MODE|
|rtx.debugView.replaceCompositeOutput|bool|False|||Replaces composite output with debug view output that is generated right after composition pass\.<br>Allows for debug view output to get the post composition pipeline applied to it, such as upscaling and postprocessing actions\)\.<br>Note any Debug Views having data set post Composite pass require this setting to be disabled to work\.<br>When disabled Debug View output is generated close to the end of RTX pipeline \(after postprocessing and upscaling\)\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_DEBUG\_VIEW\_REPLACE\_COMPOSITE\_OUTPUT|
|rtx.debugView.samplerType|int|2|||Sampler type for debug views that sample from a texture \(applies only to a subset of debug views\)\.<br>Supported types are: 0 = Nearest, 1 = Normalized Nearest, 2 = Normalized Linear|
|rtx.debugView.showFirstGBufferHit|bool|False|||Show information of the first hit surface\.<br>|
|rtx.defaultToAdvancedUI|bool|False|||Whether to default to the Advanced UI when opening the developer menu\.|
|rtx.demodulate.demodulateRoughness|bool|True|||Demodulate roughness to improve specular details\.|
|rtx.demodulate.demodulateRoughnessOffset|float|0.1|||Strength of roughness demodulation, lower values are stronger\.|
|rtx.demodulate.directLightBoilingThreshold|float|5|||Remove direct light sample when its luminance is higher than the average one multiplied by this threshold \.|
|rtx.demodulate.enableDirectLightBoilingFilter|bool|True|||Boiling filter removing direct light sample when its luminance is too high\.|
|rtx.denoiseDirectAndIndirectLightingSeparately|bool|True|||Denoising quality, high uses separate denoising of direct and indirect lighting for higher quality at the cost of performance\.<br>Environment variable: DXVK\_DENOISE\_DIRECT\_AND\_INDIRECT\_LIGHTING\_SEPARATELY|
|rtx.denoiser.maxDirectHitTContribution|float|-1||||
|rtx.denoiserIndirectMode|int|14|||Environment variable: DXVK\_DENOISER\_INDIRECT\_NRD\_MODE|
|rtx.denoiserMode|int|14|||Environment variable: DXVK\_DENOISER\_NRD\_MODE|
|rtx.di.confidenceGradientPower|float|8||||
|rtx.di.confidenceGradientScale|float|6||||
|rtx.di.confidenceHistoryLength|float|8||||
|rtx.di.confidenceHitDistanceSensitivity|float|300||||
|rtx.di.disocclusionFrames|int|8||||
|rtx.di.disocclusionSamples|int|4|||The number of spatial reuse samples in disocclusion areas\.|
|rtx.di.enableBestLightSampling|bool|True|||Whether to include a single best light from the previous frame's pixel neighborhood into initial sampling\.|
|rtx.di.enableCrossPortalLight|bool|True||||
|rtx.di.enableDenoiserConfidence|bool|True||||
|rtx.di.enableDenoiserGradient|bool|True|||Enable gradient calculation, which is used by confidence calculation and GI sample validation\.|
|rtx.di.enableDiscardEnlargedPixels|bool|True||||
|rtx.di.enableDiscardInvisibleSamples|bool|True|||Whether to discard reservoirs that are determined to be invisible in final shading\.|
|rtx.di.enableInitialVisibility|bool|True|||Whether to trace a visibility ray for the light sample selected in the initial sampling pass\.|
|rtx.di.enableRayTracedBiasCorrection|bool|True|||Whether to use ray traced bias correction in the spatial reuse pass\.|
|rtx.di.enableSampleStealing|bool|True|||No visibile IQ gains, but exhibits considerable perf drop \(8% in integrate pass\)\.|
|rtx.di.enableSpatialReuse|bool|True|||Whether to apply spatial reuse\.|
|rtx.di.enableTemporalBiasCorrection|bool|True||||
|rtx.di.enableTemporalReuse|bool|True|||Whether to apply temporal reuse\.|
|rtx.di.gradientFilterPasses|int|4||||
|rtx.di.gradientHitDistanceSensitivity|float|10||||
|rtx.di.initialSampleCount|int|4|||The number of lights randomly selected from the global pool to consider when selecting a light with RTXDI\.|
|rtx.di.maxHistoryLength|int|4|||Maximum age of reservoirs for temporal reuse\.|
|rtx.di.minimumConfidence|float|0.1||||
|rtx.di.permutationSamplingNthFrame|int|0|||Apply permutation sampling when \(frameIdx % this == 0\), 0 means off\.|
|rtx.di.spatialSamples|int|2|||The number of spatial reuse samples in converged areas\.|
|rtx.di.stealBoundaryPixelSamplesWhenOutsideOfScreen|bool|True|||Steal screen boundary samples when a hit point is outside the screen\.|
|rtx.disableAMDSwitchableGraphics|bool|True|||A flag indicating if Remix should attempt to disable AMD's switchable graphics Vulkan layer \(VK\_LAYER\_AMD\_swichable\_graphics\)\.<br>Due to how some older AMD drivers filter devices exposed to Vulkan it is possible for Remix to see no valid GPUs on a machine when using an integerated AMD GPU with a dedicated Nvidia GPU \(for instance a laptop\)\.<br>This is because on such machines both Nvidia Optimus and AMD switchable graphics attempt to filter the device list to promote their respective GPUs, but rather than leaving at least one device all end up filtered out\.<br>To work around this issue, Remix can attempt to disable the AMD switchable graphics layer which should eliminate this buggy filtering\. As such, this option should generally remain enabled\.<br>If this causes an undesired GPU to be selected \(e\.g\. if for some reason you want to force Remix to run on an integerated AMD GPU via the switchable graphics layer\), then this option should be disabled\.|
|rtx.displacement.binarySteps|int|5|||SCCR: the maximum number of steps that refine the surface crossing a march found\. The last step is a secant \(false\-position\) step, so 5 is about as precise as 6 plain bisections\. With rtx\.displacement\.enableStepLod, refinement stops once the crossing is within half a pixel footprint\.|
|rtx.displacement.coneMap.bakeNow|bool|False|||SCCR: changing this from false to true starts a bake, as the Bake now button in the Displacement settings does: every queued cone map is generated at rtx\.displacement\.coneMap\.bakeTexelsPerFrame until the queue is empty\. Maps that come into view later use the normal budget\. The runtime does not reset it, so a host must set it back to false before requesting another bake\. Not saved\.|
|rtx.displacement.coneMap.bakeTexelsPerFrame|int|262144|||SCCR: the number of cone map texels generated per frame during a bake \(Bake now, or rtx\.displacement\.coneMap\.bakeNow\), in 64x64 tiles\. The default of 64 tiles generates a 2048x2048 map in 16 frames, at a few ms of GPU time per frame\. Work is split into short dispatches, so a bake cannot cause a GPU timeout\.|
|rtx.displacement.coneMap.cacheMaxMB|int|1024|||SCCR: the cone cache is trimmed to this size in MB at startup, oldest files first\.|
|rtx.displacement.coneMap.enable|bool|True|||SCCR: generates a combined height and cone texture for the height maps of displaced materials\. Only in SCCR mode \(rtx\.displacement\.mode = 3\)\. The other modes use the source height textures and pay nothing\.|
|rtx.displacement.coneMap.enableDiskCache|bool|True|||SCCR: keeps generated cones on disk, keyed by the height map's hash, its size and the generation settings, so each map is generated only once\.|
|rtx.displacement.coneMap.generateTexelsPerFrame|int|16384|||SCCR: the number of cone map texels generated per frame, in 64x64 tiles \(at least one tile\), so generation never stalls a frame\. The default of 4 tiles generates a 2048x2048 map in 256 frames, about 4 seconds at 60 fps\. Each map is generated once \(see rtx\.displacement\.coneMap\.enableDiskCache\), and the PomConeMaps GPU stage shows the cost\. Until a map is generated, coarse cones let the cone march work anyway\.|
|rtx.displacement.coneMap.maxResolution|int|4096|||SCCR: height maps larger than this on either side get no cone map and are marched linearly\.|
|rtx.displacement.coneMap.nearRadius|int|2|||SCCR: the radius in texels \(1 to 32\) of the near field, where cone generation walks each candidate ray \(see rtx\.displacement\.coneMap\.rayStepsPerCandidate\)\. A larger radius widens the cones a little, which makes the march slightly faster, at a much higher generation cost\. On maps that are not square powers of two, a window of at least 8 texels is used\.|
|rtx.displacement.coneMap.quality|int|0|||SCCR: which cones the march uses\. 0 = Full: generated cones, with coarse cones until a map's generation is done\. 1 = CoarseOnly, for comparison: every map gets coarse cones \(one cone per 8x8 texels\) and generation pauses\. Maps that cannot have coarse cones \(not square powers of two, or a single 64x64 tile\) march linearly\. Coarse cones are never written to the disk cache\. Switching back to Full restores the generated cones from the disk cache, or resumes generation\. Uses no extra memory\.|
|rtx.displacement.coneMap.rayStepsPerCandidate|int|4|||SCCR: how many one\-texel steps cone generation walks along each near\-field candidate ray \(0 to 64\) to find where it leaves the height field\. Fewer steps give narrower cones and a slower march, but never wrong ones\. 0 gives conservative cones \(no point of the height field inside any cone\), which generate fastest, need a few percent more march steps and also allow shell\-traced self\-shadows to use the cones\. A change applies to maps generated afterwards\.|
|rtx.displacement.coneShadowSteps|int|8|||SCCR: the maximum number of cone steps of a self\-shadow march\. After that it continues in linear steps, as rtx\.displacement\.coneSteps does\.|
|rtx.displacement.coneSteps|int|16|||SCCR: the maximum number of cone steps per march\. After that, and where a texel has no cone yet, the march continues in linear steps like RaymarchPOM\. Higher values resolve deep relief better at a higher GPU cost\.|
|rtx.displacement.curvatureScale|float|1|||SCCR: the strength of rtx\.displacement\.enableCurvatureCorrection\. 1 follows the vertex normals \(a circular arc between two vertices\)\. 0 is the flat triangle\.|
|rtx.displacement.displacementFactor|float|1|||Scaling factor for all displacement maps|
|rtx.displacement.displacementInFactor|float|1|||Scale factor for inwards displacement|
|rtx.displacement.displacementOutFactor|float|1|||Scale factor for outwards displacement|
|rtx.displacement.enableCurvatureCorrection|bool|True|||SCCR: lays the relief on the smooth surface that the vertex normals describe instead of on the flat triangle, by bending the march ray\. Restores rounded parallax on smoothly shaded low\-poly meshes such as rocks\. The bend is limited to half the displacement depth\.<br>Needs vertex normals \(no effect on flat\-shaded meshes\) and rtx\.displacement\.enableStableFrame\.|
|rtx.displacement.enableDepthOffset|bool|True|||SCCR: writes the depth, linear view depth and motion vectors of a displaced primary surface at the relief point the march found instead of at the mesh\. Upscalers such as DLSS then reproject the carved texels with their own depth, which stops deep relief from smearing or swimming when the camera moves\. Shading positions, lighting distances and self\-shadows still use the mesh\.|
|rtx.displacement.enableDirectLighting|bool|True|||Whether direct lighting accounts for displacement mapping|
|rtx.displacement.enableIndirectHit|bool|False|||Whether indirect ray hits account for displacement mapping \(Enabling this is expensive\.  Without it, non\-perfect reflections of displaced objects will not show displacement\.\)|
|rtx.displacement.enableIndirectLighting|bool|True|||Whether indirect lighting accounts for displacement mapping|
|rtx.displacement.enableNEECache|bool|True|||Whether the NEE cache accounts for displacement mapping|
|rtx.displacement.enablePSR|bool|False|||Enable PSR \(perfect reflections\) for materials with displacement\.  Rays that have been perfectly reflected off a POM surface will not collide correctly with other parts of that same surface\.|
|rtx.displacement.enableReSTIRGI|bool|True|||Whether ReSTIR GI accounts for displacement mapping|
|rtx.displacement.enableStableFrame|bool|False|||SCCR: marches in the triangle's own tangent frame \(its geometric normal and the UV directions of its edges\) instead of a frame built around the interpolated vertex normal\. This keeps the relief attached to the mesh when the camera moves, instead of sliding across smoothly shaded low\-poly triangles\. Self\-shadows use the same normal\.|
|rtx.displacement.enableStepLod|bool|True|||SCCR: skips march steps that cannot change the result\. No step is shorter than one pixel footprint of UV travel, refinement stops at half a footprint, and a march that would move less than a quarter of a footprint takes a single sample\. Saves GPU time on minified height maps and head\-on views\. Self\-shadow marches use the height map texel size instead of the pixel footprint\.|
|rtx.displacement.enableWorldUnitDepth|bool|True|||SCCR: keeps the displacement depth constant in world units, whatever the texture tiling\. Material depths are given in UV units, so without this a densely tiled surface gets shallow relief and a stretched one deep relief\. The depth is scaled by rtx\.displacement\.referenceWorldUnitsPerUv divided by the triangle's world units per UV, limited by rtx\.displacement\.maxWorldUnitDepthScale\. Self\-shadows use the same depth\.|
|rtx.displacement.indirectShadowFootprintScale|float|1|||SCCR: scales the ray cone footprint used to choose the mip of indirect\-light self\-shadows \(rtx\.displacement\.indirectShadowSteps\)\. Larger values resolve less detail on secondary surfaces\. 0 uses the step count alone\.|
|rtx.displacement.indirectShadowSteps|int|2|||SCCR: the number of linear steps used for displacement self\-shadows of indirect light \(secondary surfaces, the radiance caches, ReSTIR GI and the first indirect bounce\)\. These marches sample a coarser mip and skip refinement, which saves GPU time\. Direct light on primary surfaces keeps full detail\.<br>0 marches every self\-shadow at full detail\.|
|rtx.displacement.maxIterations|int|64|||The max number of times the POM raymarch will iterate\.|
|rtx.displacement.maxWorldUnitDepthScale|float|4|||SCCR: the largest factor rtx\.displacement\.enableWorldUnitDepth applies to a material's depth, up or down\. 4 keeps the depth between a quarter and 4 times the authored depth\.|
|rtx.displacement.mode|int|3|||The displacement \(parallax\) algorithm\.<br>RaymarchPOM: advances the ray in linear steps until it is below the height field\.<br>QuadtreePOM: uses special mipmaps that store maximum instead of average heights, as a quadtree\.<br>SCCR: relaxed cone stepping on cone maps that are generated from the height maps at load time \(rtx\.displacement\.coneMap\.\*\), followed by a binary search\. Where no cone map is available yet, it marches in linear steps like RaymarchPOM\. Shell tracing \(rtx\.displacement\.shell\.enable\) keeps deep relief from sliding\.<br>Values: 0: Off, 1: RaymarchPOM, 2: QuadtreePOM, 3: SCCR\.|
|rtx.displacement.referenceWorldUnitsPerUv|float|256|||SCCR: the texture density, in world units per UV unit \(the world size of one texture repeat\), at which rtx\.displacement\.enableWorldUnitDepth leaves material depths unchanged\. This depends on the game\. Set it with the SCCR World Depth Scale debug view, which shows green where a surface is at this density, red where it is tiled more densely and blue where it is stretched\.|
|rtx.displacement.shell.buildTrianglesPerFrame|int|500000|||SCCR shell tracing: the number of triangles of shell data built per frame \(at least one mesh\), so loading many meshes at once never stalls a frame\. A mesh uses plain SCCR until its data is built\.|
|rtx.displacement.shell.enable|bool|False|||SCCR shell tracing: marches displaced static meshes through prisms between the mesh and an inner shell offset along the vertex normals, so deep relief stays attached to the mesh across triangle edges instead of sliding\. Builds per\-mesh data \(see the SCCR Shell Edges debug view\) and marches primary and reflected \(PSR\) hits closer than rtx\.displacement\.shell\.maxDistance\. The outline stays the mesh's\.<br>While on, SCCR ignores the zero plane \(the mesh is the top of the relief\) and always uses world\-unit depth, so rtx\.displacement\.referenceWorldUnitsPerUv must be above 0\. SCCR mode only\. Skinned and vertex\-animated meshes use plain SCCR\. Turning it off, or leaving SCCR mode, frees the data\.|
|rtx.displacement.shell.foldGuard|bool|True|||SCCR shell tracing: limits the shell depth at vertices where the inner shell would fold over itself \(at sharp convex corners, thin slabs and small features\), so the relief gets shallower there instead of breaking up\. When off, the full depth is used everywhere, and marches that reach a folded prism stop there \(cyan in the SCCR Shell Result debug view\)\.|
|rtx.displacement.shell.maxDistance|float|2000|||SCCR shell tracing: the hit distance, in world units, beyond which pixels use plain SCCR instead of the shell march\. Nearer pixels also use plain SCCR where the whole relief depth is less than a pixel\. 0 turns the march off, but the data is still built\. The SCCR quality levels and graphics presets set it in proportion to rtx\.displacement\.referenceWorldUnitsPerUv \(2000 at 256 for High\)\.|
|rtx.displacement.shell.maxMB|int|128|||SCCR shell tracing: the most memory, in MB, the per\-mesh shell data may use \(12 bytes per triangle and 12 per vertex\)\. A mesh that would exceed it uses plain SCCR, and is tried again once memory is freed\.|
|rtx.displacement.shell.maxPrisms|int|32|||SCCR shell tracing: the most neighbouring prisms one march crosses into\. Grazing views near outlines at large depths cross the most, about 20 on a smooth boulder\. A march that runs out while rising above the relief counts as an outline\. Otherwise the pixel uses plain SCCR \(magenta in the SCCR Shell Result debug view\)\.|
|rtx.displacement.shell.maxShadowPrisms|int|4|||SCCR shell tracing: the most neighbouring prisms one self\-shadow march crosses into on its way to the light \(at most 255\)\. A shadow ray that needs more counts as lit from there\.|
|rtx.displacement.shell.maxSteps|int|128|||SCCR shell tracing: the most steps one march takes, counting every solve, crossing and refinement step\. A march that runs out is handled like one that runs out of prisms \(rtx\.displacement\.shell\.maxPrisms\)\.|
|rtx.displacement.shell.shadows|bool|True|||SCCR shell tracing: direct\-light self\-shadows of shell pixels march towards the light through the same prisms as the view ray, so crevice shadows stay on the carved relief\. Indirect light keeps the cheaper texture\-space self\-shadow\. When off, direct light uses the texture\-space self\-shadow too\.|
|rtx.displacement.shell.weldEpsilon|float|0|||SCCR shell tracing: how close two vertex positions must be to count as one when triangles are joined to their neighbours, as a fraction of the mesh's bounding box diagonal\. 0 joins only identical positions, which suits meshes whose split vertices \(hard edges and UV seams\) copy the position exactly\. Raise it \(1e\-6 to 1e\-4\) for meshes that show red edges or magenta corners in the SCCR Shell Edges debug view where the surface is closed\. Too much merges real details\. Changing it rebuilds all shell data\.|
|rtx.displacement.stepLodScale|float|1|||SCCR: steps per pixel footprint for rtx\.displacement\.enableStepLod\. Larger values take finer steps \(2 takes half\-footprint steps\), smaller values are faster\.|
|rtx.displacement.zeroPlane|float|-1|||SCCR: the height\-map value \(0 = black, 1 = white\) that lies on the mesh surface, overriding the material's split of its depth into displace\-in and displace\-out\. The total depth is kept\. 0\.5 puts mid\-grey on the mesh, so white rises above it and black sinks below\. Use it when a host passes no displace\-out depth and the whole relief sinks into the mesh\.<br>A negative value uses the material's own split\.|
|rtx.dlfg.enable|bool|True|||Enables DLSS 3\.0 frame generation which generates interpolated frames to increase framerate at the cost of slightly more latency\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_DLFG\_ENABLE|
|rtx.dlfg.enablePresentMetering|bool|True|||Use hardware present metering for DLSS 4\.0 frame generation instead of CPU pacing\.|
|rtx.dlfg.litePreset|bool|False|||Selects the DLSS Frame Generation preset\. True: Preset C \(lightweight model\)\. False: Preset A \(standard model\)\. Switching recreates Frame Generation without restarting\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_DLFG\_LITE\_PRESET|
|rtx.dlfg.maxInterpolatedFrames|int|2|1|6|For DLSS 4\.5 frame generation, controls the number of interpolated frames for each rendered frame\. Ignored for DLSS 3\.0\.|
|rtx.dlss.preset|int|0|||Render preset for DLSS Super Resolution\. Default = 0 \(DLSS picks the best preset\), J = 10, K = 11, L = 12, M = 13\.|
|rtx.dlssEnhancementDirectLightMaxValue|float|10|||The maximum strength of direct lighting enhancement\.|
|rtx.dlssEnhancementDirectLightPower|float|0.7|||The overall strength of direct lighting enhancement\.|
|rtx.dlssEnhancementIndirectLightMaxValue|float|1.5|||The maximum strength of indirect lighting enhancement\.|
|rtx.dlssEnhancementIndirectLightMinRoughness|float|0.3|||The reference roughness in indirect lighting enhancement\.|
|rtx.dlssEnhancementIndirectLightPower|float|1|||The overall strength of indirect lighting enhancement\.|
|rtx.dlssEnhancementMode|int|1|||The enhancement filter type\. Valid values: \<Normal Difference=1, Laplacian=0\>\. Normal difference mode provides more normal detail at the cost of some noise\. Laplacian mode is less aggressive\.|
|rtx.dlssPreset|int|1|||Combined DLSS Preset for quickly controlling Upscaling, Frame Interpolation and Latency Reduction\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_DLSS\_PRESET|
|rtx.dof.autoFocusDeadZone|float|0.03|||Relative optical\-power change below which auto\-focus holds the current focus distance\.|
|rtx.dof.autoFocusEnable|bool|False|||Measure and smoothly track the depth\-of\-field focus distance from the screen\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_DOF\_AUTO\_FOCUS\_ENABLE|
|rtx.dof.autoFocusFarTauScale|float|3|||Multiplier applied to the auto\-focus smoothing time constant when focus moves to a farther distance\.|
|rtx.dof.autoFocusOffset|float|0|||An offset in world units added to the measured auto\-focus distance\.|
|rtx.dof.autoFocusPointX|float|0.5|||Normalized horizontal screen position used for auto\-focus\.|
|rtx.dof.autoFocusPointY|float|0.5|||Normalized vertical screen position used for auto\-focus\.|
|rtx.dof.autoFocusRegionRadius|float|0.02|||Normalized radius \(fraction of the smaller image dimension\) of the disk sampled around the auto\-focus point\.|
|rtx.dof.autoFocusTau|float|0.25|||Auto\-focus smoothing time constant in seconds when focus moves to a nearer distance\.|
|rtx.dof.bokehFilterStrength|float|0.25|||Width of the reconstruction tent used when the half resolution bokeh layers are upsampled\. 0: plain bilinear, 1: a full half\-resolution texel\.|
|rtx.dof.bokehMinIntensity|float|1|||Intensity of the centre of the bokeh disc relative to its rim\. 0: strongest rim emphasis \(optical\-vignetting look\), 1: even disc\.|
|rtx.dof.dofEnable|bool|False|||Enable the depth\-of\-field effect\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_DOF\_ENABLE|
|rtx.dof.edgeAwareUpsample|bool|True|||Aligns the depth\-of\-field blur boundary to the upscaled colour edges\. Without it the boundary follows the render resolution and stair\-steps along silhouettes whenever DLSS or another upscaler is active\. Costs a few extra texture reads per pixel\. At native resolution it makes no difference\.|
|rtx.dof.excludeViewModel|bool|True|||Keeps the view model \(the player's weapon and hands\) sharp\. The view model sits a few centimetres from the lens, so a physically correct lens would blur it the most and spread its blur across much of the screen\. This normally costs nothing and often saves GPU time\. Turn it off to frame a shot with the weapon deliberately out of focus\.|
|rtx.dof.fNumber|float|2.8|||Lens aperture f\-number\. Higher values deepen the depth of field, lower values produce more blur\.|
|rtx.dof.focalLength|float|100|||Lens focal length in millimeters\. Longer lenses produce a shallower depth of field\.|
|rtx.dof.focusDistance|float|5|||Manual depth\-of\-field focus distance in world units\.|
|rtx.dof.maxBlurRadius|float|16|||The largest blur radius, in pixels at 1080p\. It scales with the output height and is limited to 128 output pixels, so at higher resolutions the limit applies sooner \(about 86 at 1600p and 59 at 2160p\)\.|
|rtx.dof.sampleCount|int|96|||The most half\-resolution bokeh samples per layer\. The number of samples grows with the blur radius up to this limit, so it caps quality rather than setting a fixed cost per pixel\. Values from 1 to 31 act as 32\. 0 disables the effect\.|
|rtx.drawCallRange|int2|0, 2147483647||||
|rtx.dumpAllInstancesOnFrame|int|-1|||If set, and running in a REMIX\_DEVELOPMENT build, this will dump all active instances to the log on the specified frame\.|
|rtx.dust.anisotropy|float|0.5|||Anisotropy of the particles for lighting purposes\.|
|rtx.dust.enable|bool|False|||Enables dust particle simulation and rendering\.|
|rtx.dust.gravityForce|float|-0.5|||Net influence of gravity acting on each particle \(meters per second squared\)\.|
|rtx.dust.maxParticleLife|float|6|||Maximum lifetime \(in seconds\) to give to a particle when spawned\.|
|rtx.dust.maxParticleSize|float|3|||Maximum size \(in pixels at 1080p reference resolution\) to give to a particle when spawned\.|
|rtx.dust.maxSpawnDistance|float|400|||Maximum distance in world space from camera to spawn particles\.|
|rtx.dust.maxSpeed|float|3|||Maximum speed of a particle in world space\.|
|rtx.dust.minParticleLife|float|3|||Minimum lifetime \(in seconds\) to give to a particle when spawned\.|
|rtx.dust.minParticleSize|float|1|||Minimum size \(in pixels at 1080p reference resolution\) to give to a particle when spawned\.|
|rtx.dust.minSpawnDistance|float|20|||Minimum distance in world space from camera to spawn particles\.|
|rtx.dust.numberOfParticles|int|1000000|||Maximum number of particles to simulate simultaneously\.|
|rtx.dust.opacity|float|0.5|||Opacity of the particles\.|
|rtx.dust.rotationSpeed|float|5|||How quickly the particle is rotating \(this primarily only affects light interaction\)\.|
|rtx.dust.timeScale|float|1|||Time modifier, can be used to slow/speed up time\.|
|rtx.dust.turbulenceAmplitude|float|5|||How much turbulence influences the force of a particle\.|
|rtx.dust.turbulenceFrequency|float|0.05|||The rate of change of turbulence forces\.|
|rtx.dust.useTurbulence|bool|True|||Enable turbulence simulation\.|
|rtx.effectLightColor|float3|1, 1, 1|||Colour of the effect light, if not using plasma ball mode\.  Effect lights can be attached to materials from the remix runtime menu, using the \`Add Light to Texture\` texture tag in game setup\.|
|rtx.effectLightIntensity|float|1|||The intensity of the effect light\.  Effect lights can be attached to materials from the remix runtime menu, using the \`Add Light to Texture\` texture tag in game setup\.|
|rtx.effectLightPlasmaBall|bool|False|||Use plasma ball mode, in this mode the effect light color is ignored\.  Effect lights can be attached to materials from the remix runtime menu, using the \`Add Light to Texture\` texture tag in game setup\.|
|rtx.effectLightRadius|float|5|||The sphere radius of the effect light\.  Effect lights can be attached to materials from the remix runtime menu, using the \`Add Light to Texture\` texture tag in game setup\.|
|rtx.emissiveBlendOverrideEmissiveIntensity|float|0.2|0|65504|The emissive intensity to use when the emissive blend override is enabled\. Adjust this if particles for example look overly bright globally\.|
|rtx.emissiveBlendOverrideScaleByMaterial|bool|False|||Multiplies the emissive blend override intensity by the material's own emissive intensity, for materials that enable emission\. Lets an API host give each additive effect its own brightness, which the override otherwise replaces with one global value\. Materials without emission are unchanged\.|
|rtx.emissiveIntensity|float|1|0||A global scale on all emissive intensities\. Per\-material emissive intensities are the normal way to set emission, but this option is useful for debugging without authoring materials\.<br>To convert a game's emissive units to physical units, use rtx\.hostCalibration\.emissiveOffsetEv instead and leave this at 1\.|
|rtx.enableAlphaBlend|bool|True|||Enable rendering alpha blended geometry, used for partial opacity and other blending effects on various surfaces in many games\.|
|rtx.enableAlphaTest|bool|True|||Enable rendering alpha tested geometry, used for cutout style opacity in some games\.|
|rtx.enableAlwaysCalculateAABB|bool|False|||Calculate an Axis Aligned Bounding Box for every draw call\.<br> This may improve instance tracking across frames for skinned and vertex shaded calls\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_ALWAYS\_CALCULATE\_AABB|
|rtx.enableBillboardOrientationCorrection|bool|True||||
|rtx.enableBreakIntoDebuggerOnPressingB|bool|False|||Enables a break into a debugger at the start of InjectRTX\(\) on a press of key 'B'\.<br>If debugger is not attached at the time, it will wait until a debugger is attached and break into it then\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_BREAK\_INTO\_DEBUGGER\_ON\_PRESSING\_B|
|rtx.enableCrashHotkey|bool|False|||Arms the crash hotkey feature\. When enabled, pressing the crash hotkey combination \(Ctrl\+Shift\+Alt\+K by default\) will trigger a deliberate crash\.<br>This option is only available in development builds and is intended for testing crash handling and crash dump generation\.<br>The armed state is indicated by a red warning overlay on screen\. This setting is not saved to config files but can be set manually in rtx\.conf\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_ENABLE\_CRASH\_HOTKEY|
|rtx.enableCulling|bool|True|||Enable front/backface culling for opaque objects\. Objects with alpha blend or alpha test are not culled\.|
|rtx.enableCullingInSecondaryRays|bool|False|||Enable front/backface culling for opaque objects\. Objects with alpha blend or alpha test are not culled\.  Only applies in secondary rays, defaults to off\.  Generally helps with light bleeding from objects that aren't watertight\.|
|rtx.enableDLSSEnhancement|bool|True|||Enhances lighting details when DLSS is on\.|
|rtx.enableDecalMaterialBlending|bool|True|||A flag to enable or disable material blending on decals\.<br>This should generally always be enabled when decals are in use as this allows decals to be blended down on to the surface they sit slightly above which results in more convincing decals rendering\.|
|rtx.enableDirectAlphaBlendShadows|bool|True|||Calculate shadows for semi\-transparent materials \(alpha blended\) in direct lighting\. In engineering terms: include OBJECT\_MASK\_ALPHA\_BLEND into primary visibility rays\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_ENABLE\_DIRECT\_ALPHABLEND\_SHADOWS|
|rtx.enableDirectLighting|bool|True|||Enables direct lighting \(lighting directly from lights on to a surface\) on surfaces when set to true, otherwise disables it\.|
|rtx.enableDirectTranslucentShadows|bool|False|||Calculate coloured shadows for translucent materials \(i\.e\. glass, water\) in direct lighting\. In engineering terms: include OBJECT\_MASK\_TRANSLUCENT into primary visibility rays\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_ENABLE\_DIRECT\_TRANSLUCENT\_SHADOWS|
|rtx.enableEmissiveBlendEmissiveOverride|bool|True|||Override typical material emissive information on draw calls with any emissive blending modes to emulate their original look more accurately\.|
|rtx.enableEmissiveBlendModeTranslation|bool|True|||Treat incoming semi/additive D3D blend modes as emissive\.|
|rtx.enableExternalPreservePath|bool|False|||Lets draws submitted through the Remix API reuse the instances they produced on earlier frames when nothing about them has changed, instead of processing every draw again each frame\. Saves CPU time in API hosts\.<br>Only applies to single\-submesh draws without USD replacements or particles\. A wrong reuse shows as frozen or stale geometry, so confirm it per game\. The \[ExternalPreserve\] log line shows the preserved and resubmitted counts\.|
|rtx.enableFallbackLightShaping|bool|False|||Enables light shaping on the fallback light \(only used for non\-Distant light types\)\.|
|rtx.enableFallbackLightViewPrimaryAxis|bool|False|||Enables usage of the camera's view axis as the primary axis for the fallback light's shaping \(only used for non \- Distant light types\)\. Typically the shaping primary axis may be specified directly, but if desired it may be set to the camera's view axis for a "flashlight" effect\.|
|rtx.enableFirstBounceLobeProbabilityDithering|bool|True|||A flag to enable or disable screen\-space probability dithering on the first indirect lobe sampled\.<br>Generally sampling a diffuse, specular or other lobe relies on a random number generated against the probability of sampling each lobe, effectively focusing more rays/paths on lobes which matter more\.<br>This can cause issues however with denoisers which do not handle sparse stochastic signals \(like those from path tracing\) well as they may be expecting a more "complete" signal like those used in simpler branching ray tracing setups\.<br>To help solve this issue this option uses a temporal screenspace dithering based on the probability rather than a purely random choice to determine which lobe to sample from on the first indirect bounce\.<br>This as a result helps ensure there will always be a diffuse or specular sample within the dithering pattern's area and should help the denoising resolve a more stable result\.|
|rtx.enableFog|bool|True||||
|rtx.enableGpuScreenOverlay|bool|True|||Lets a native D3D11 host pass its UI render targets to Remix with remixapi\_DrawScreenOverlayD3D11, as a GPU copy with no CPU readback\.<br>When off, that call is refused and clears its layers, and the host falls back to the slower CPU overlay path \(DrawScreenOverlay\)\.|
|rtx.enableIndexBufferMemoization|bool|True|||CPU performance optimization, should generally be enabled\.  Will reduce main thread time by caching processIndexBuffer operations and reusing when possible, this will come at the expense of some CPU RAM\.|
|rtx.enableIndirectAlphaBlendShadows|bool|True|||Calculate shadows for semi\-transparent \(alpha blended\) objects in indirect lighting \(i\.e\. reflections and GI\)\. In engineering terms: include OBJECT\_MASK\_ALPHA\_BLEND into secondary visibility rays\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_ENABLE\_INDIRECT\_ALPHABLEND\_SHADOWS|
|rtx.enableIndirectTranslucentShadows|bool|False|||Calculate coloured shadows for translucent materials \(i\.e\. glass, water\) in indirect lighting \(i\.e\. reflections and GI\)\. In engineering terms: include OBJECT\_MASK\_TRANSLUCENT into secondary visibility rays\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_ENABLE\_INDIRECT\_TRANSLUCENT\_SHADOWS|
|rtx.enableInputQueuePing|bool|False|||When enabled, Remix posts a WM\_NULL message to the game window every rtx\.inputQueuePingIntervalMs milliseconds so Windows keeps servicing the window's input queue and does not flag the game as not responding while it presents without focus\.<br>This setting is read when the swapchain is created; changing it will not take effect at runtime\.|
|rtx.enableInstanceDebuggingTools|bool|False|||NOTE: This will disable temporal correllation for instances, but allow the use of instance developer debug tools|
|rtx.enableLegacyRectLightConeShaping|bool|False|||If true, restores the legacy cos\(angle\) \* aspectRatio formula for RectLight cone shaping\. Enable this only to preserve the look of existing scenes that were authored against the legacy behavior\. Note this can silently kill the light at high aspect ratios\.|
|rtx.enableMultiStageTextureFactorBlending|bool|True|||Support texture factor blending in stage 1~7\. Currently only support 1 additional blending stage, more than 1 additional blending stages will be ignored\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_ENABLE\_MULTI\_STAGE\_TEXTURE\_FACTOR\_BLENDING|
|rtx.enableNearPlaneOverride|bool|False|||A flag to enable or disable the Camera's near plane override feature\.<br>Since the camera is not used directly for ray tracing the near plane the application uses typically does not matter, but for certain matrix\-based operations \(such as temporal reprojection or voxel grid projection\) it is still relevant\.<br>The issue arises when geometry is ray traced that is behind where the chosen Camera's near plane is located, typically common on viewmodels especially with how they are ray traced, causing graphical artifacts and other issues\.<br>This option helps correct this issue by overriding the near plane value to else \(usually smaller\) to sit behind the objects in question \(such as the view model\)\. As such this option should usually be enabled on games with viewmodels\.<br>Do note that when adjusting the near plane the larger the relative magnitude gap between the near and far plane the worse the precision of matrix operations will be, so the near plane should be set as high as possible even when overriding\.|
|rtx.enablePSRR|bool|True|||A flag to enable or disable reflection PSR \(Primary Surface Replacement\)\.<br>When enabled this feature allows higher quality mirror\-like reflections in special cases by replacing the G\-Buffer's surface with the reflected surface\.<br>Should usually be enabled for the sake of quality as almost all applications will utilize it in the form of glass or mirrors\.|
|rtx.enablePSTR|bool|True|||A flag to enable or disable transmission PSR \(Primary Surface Replacement\)\.<br>When enabled this feature allows higher quality glass\-like refraction in special cases by replacing the G\-Buffer's surface with the refracted surface\.<br>Should usually be enabled for the sake of quality as almost all applications will utilize it in the form of glass\.|
|rtx.enablePSTROutgoingSplitApproximation|bool|True|||Enable transmission PSR on outgoing transmission events such as leaving translucent materials \(rather than respecting no\-split path PSR rule\)\.<br>Typically this results in better looking glass when enabled \(at the cost of accuracy due to ignoring non\-TIR inter\-reflections within the glass itself\)\.|
|rtx.enablePSTRSecondaryIncidentSplitApproximation|bool|True|||Enable transmission PSR on secondary incident transmission events such as entering a translucent material on an already\-transmitted path \(rather than respecting no\-split path PSR rule\)\.<br>Typically this results in better looking glass when enabled \(at the cost accuracy due to ignoring reflections off of glass seen through glass for example\)\.|
|rtx.enablePortalFadeInEffect|bool|False||||
|rtx.enablePresentThrottle|bool|False|||A flag to enable or disable present throttling, when set to true a sleep for a time specified by the throttle delay will be inserted into the DXVK presentation thread\.<br>Useful to manually reduce the framerate if the application is running too fast or to reduce GPU power usage during development to keep temperatures down\.<br>Should not be enabled in anything other than development situations\.|
|rtx.enablePreservePath|bool|True|||When true, Remix attempts to identify draw calls whose state has not changed since last frame and re\-use the previous<br>frame's translation, rather than retranslating the draw call into raytrace\-ready scene data\.<br>When false, every submit uses full dynamic geometry and instance processing \(drawReplacements / processDrawCallState\)\.<br>Disable for debugging or compatibility when suspecting preserve\-path regressions\.|
|rtx.enableProbabilisticUnorderedResolveInIndirectRays|bool|True|||A flag to enable or disable probabilistic unordered resolve approximations in indirect rays\.<br>This flag speeds up the unordered resolve for indirect rays by probabilistically deciding when to perform unordered resolve or not\.  Must have both unordered resolve and unordered resolve in indirect rays enabled for this to take effect\.<br>This option should be enabled by default as it can significantly improve performance on some hardware\.  In rare cases it may come at the cost of some quality for particles and decals in reflections\.<br>Note that even with this option enabled, unordered resolve approximations are only done on the first indirect bounce for the sake of performance overall\.|
|rtx.enableRayReconstruction|bool|True|||Enables DLSS ray reconstruction, an AI\-based denoiser designed for real time ray tracing\.<br>Environment variable: DXVK\_RAY\_RECONSTRUCTION|
|rtx.enableRaytracing|bool|True|||Globally enables or disables ray tracing\. When set to false the original game should render mostly as it would in DXVK typically\.<br>Some artifacts may still appear however compared to the original game either due to issues with the underlying DXVK translation or issues in Remix itself\.<br>Environment variable: DXVK\_ENABLE\_RAYTRACING|
|rtx.enableRectLightConeShapingRatioScaling|bool|False|||If true, scales the RectLight cone half\-angle by the light's aspect ratio in tangent space, producing a cone that matches the rect's proportions\. When false \(default\), the cone is uniform and independent of the light's width/height\. Ignored when enableLegacyRectLightConeShaping is true\.|
|rtx.enableReplacementAssets|bool|True|||Globally enables or disables all enhanced asset replacement \(materials, meshes, lights\) functionality\.|
|rtx.enableReplacementInstancerMeshRendering|bool|True|||Enables or disables rendering GeomPointInstancer meshes using an optimized path\.<br>Requires reloading replacement assets\.|
|rtx.enableReplacementLights|bool|True|||Enables or disables enhanced light replacements\.<br>Requires replacement assets in general to be enabled to have any effect\.|
|rtx.enableReplacementMaterials|bool|True|||Enables or disables enhanced material replacements\.<br>Requires replacement assets in general to be enabled to have any effect\.|
|rtx.enableReplacementMeshes|bool|True|||Enables or disables enhanced mesh replacements\.<br>Requires replacement assets in general to be enabled to have any effect\.|
|rtx.enableRetainedPreservePath|bool|True|||Lets a Remix API retained instance that has not changed since the last frame \(same description, transform, mesh and material\) refresh its existing instance instead of being processed in full every frame\. Saves CPU time\. Only applies to single\-submesh draws without USD replacements or particles\. Independent of rtx\.enableExternalPreservePath, which covers per\-frame DrawInstance calls\.|
|rtx.enableRussianRoulette|bool|True|||A flag to enable or disable Russian Roulette, a rendering technique to give paths a chance of terminating randomly with each bounce based on their importance\.<br>This is usually useful to have enabled as it will ensure useless paths are terminated earlier while more important paths are allowed to accumulate more bounces\.<br>Furthermore this allows for the renderer to remain unbiased whereas a hard clamp on the number of bounces will introduce bias \(though this is also done in Remix for the sake of performance\)\.<br>On the other hand, randomly terminating paths too aggressively may leave threads in GPU warps without work which may hurt thread occupancy when not used with a thread\-reordering technique like SER\.<br>Additionally, Russian Roulette will always for the most part increase variance and will reduce the average path depth from whatever the current maximum path length is set to\.<br>This increase in variance will slightly impact image quality especially on scenes relying heavily on many bounces of indirect lighting, but this is usually worth it for efficiency purposes, as Russian Roulette allows each ray to reduces variance more than it would otherwise\.|
|rtx.enableSecondaryBounces|bool|True|||Enables indirect lighting \(lighting from diffuse/specular bounces to one or more other surfaces\) on surfaces when set to true, otherwise disables it\.|
|rtx.enableSeparateUnorderedApproximations|bool|True|||Use a separate loop during resolving for surfaces which can have lighting evaluated in an approximate unordered way on each path segment \(such as particles\)\.<br>This improves performance typically in how particles or decals are rendered and should usually always be enabled\.<br>Do note however the unordered nature of this resolving method may result in visual artifacts with large numbers of stacked particles due to difficulty in determining the intended order\.<br>Additionally, unordered approximations will only be done on the first indirect ray bounce \(as particles matter less in higher bounces\), and only if enabled by its corresponding setting\.|
|rtx.enableShaderExecutionReorderingInPathtracerGbuffer|bool|False|||\(Note: Hard disabled in shader code\) Enables Shader Execution Reordering \(SER\) in GBuffer Raytrace pass if SER is supported\.|
|rtx.enableShaderExecutionReorderingInPathtracerIntegrateIndirect|bool|True|||Enables Shader Execution Reordering \(SER\) in Integrate Indirect pass if SER is supported\.|
|rtx.enableSpecularMetal|bool|True|||For API materials that describe a specular/gloss roughness map \(remixapi\_MaterialInfoOpaqueSpecGlossEXT with a non\-zero metal strength\), derives a per\-pixel metalness from the map's red \(specular\) channel\. Turn it off to keep such materials dielectric, using only the constant metalness\. Roughness is unaffected\.|
|rtx.enableStochasticAlphaBlend|bool|True|||Use stochastic alpha blend\.|
|rtx.enableTransmissionApproximationInIndirectRays|bool|False|||A flag to enable transmission approximations in indirect rays\.<br>Translucent objects hit by indirect rays will not alter ray direction, just change the ray throughput\.|
|rtx.enableUnorderedEmissiveParticlesInIndirectRays|bool|False|||A flag to enable or disable unordered resolve emissive particles specifically in indirect rays\.<br>Should be enabled in higher quality rendering modes as emissive particles are fairly important in reflections, but may be disabled to skip such interactions which can improve performance on lower end hardware\.<br>Note that rtx\.enableUnorderedResolveInIndirectRays must first be enabled for this option to take any effect \(as it will control if unordered resolve is used to begin with in indirect rays\)\.<br>Environment variable: DXVK\_EMISSIVE\_INDIRECT\_PARTICLES|
|rtx.enableUnorderedResolveInIndirectRays|bool|True|||A flag to enable or disable unordered resolve approximations in indirect rays\.<br>This allows for the presence of unordered approximations in resolving to be overridden in indirect rays and as such requires separate unordered approximations to be enabled to have any effect\.<br>This option should be enabled if objects which can be resolvered in an unordered way in indirect rays are expected for higher quality in reflections, but may come at a performance cost\.<br>Note that even with this option enabled, unordered resolve approximations are only done on the first indirect bounce for the sake of performance overall\.|
|rtx.enableValidationLayerExtendedValidation|bool|False|||A flag to enable extended validation to validation layers in Vulkan\. Only takes effect if validation layers are enabled already\.<br>This flag enables GPU assisted and synchronization validation along with best practices within the Vulkan validation layers which allow for greater error\-checking capability at the cost of significant performance impact\.<br>Much like the rtx\.enableValidationLayers option, this option should only be enabled by developers during development and not be put into production builds of any project\.<br>Additionally, this setting must be set at startup and changing it will not take effect at runtime\.<br>Environment variable: DXVK\_ENABLE\_VALIDATION\_LAYER\_EXTENDED\_VALIDATION|
|rtx.enableValidationLayers|bool|False|||A flag to enable validation layers in Vulkan\. Note that in Debug builds validation layers will always be enabled and this flag will have no effect\.<br>Enabling validation layers is useful for debugging and development to catch common issues in Vulkan, but will reduce overall performance\.<br>Should only be enabled by developers during development and not put into production builds of any project\.<br>Additionally, this setting must be set at startup and changing it will not take effect at runtime\.<br>Environment variable: DXVK\_ENABLE\_VALIDATION\_LAYERS|
|rtx.enableVsync|int|2|||Controls the game's V\-Sync setting\. Native game's V\-Sync settings are ignored\.|
|rtx.eye.assumeViewTexgenModeAsEye|bool|True|||Used to detect eyes and its vectors, by assuming that a draw call with D3DTSS\_TCI\_CAMERASPACEPOSITION and specific texture transform is an eye draw call\.|
|rtx.eye.corneaSphereOffset|float|0.1|||The radius of the sphere used for eye normals on the cornea, in world units\. Larger values make the cornea look more spherical\.|
|rtx.eye.enable|bool|False|||Enable shader code for eye drawing \(eyeball normals, iris blending\)\.|
|rtx.eye.eyeWhitesAlbedoScale|float|0.5|||Brightness multiplier for the eye whites\.|
|rtx.eye.eyeballSphereOffset|float|0.18|||The radius of the sphere used for eye normals on the whites of the eyes, in world units\. Larger values give more pronounced ambient shadowing on the eyeball, which grounds the eyes in the face\.|
|rtx.eye.irisDepth|float|0.06|||How deep should the iris \(colored part of an eye\) be placed behind the cornea \(eye lens\)\. The larger the value the more distortion there is, because of the lens\.|
|rtx.eye.irisRadius|float|0.165|||Size of an iris in the iris texture\. If the iris texture is sampled outside of this radius, it's assumed that that area is a transition to the eye whites, so iris depth gradually goes to 0\.|
|rtx.eye.showOptions|bool|False|||Show eye options in the developer menu\.|
|rtx.fallbackLightAngle|float|5|0||The angular size in degrees to use for the fallback light \(used only for Distant light types\)\. Should only be within the range \[0, 180\]\.|
|rtx.fallbackLightConeAngle|float|25|0||The cone angle in degrees to use for the fallback light shaping \(used only for non\-Distant light types with shaping enabled\)\. Should only be within the range \[0, 180\]\.|
|rtx.fallbackLightConeSoftness|float|0.1|0||The cone softness to use for the fallback light shaping \(used only for non\-Distant light types with shaping enabled\)\.|
|rtx.fallbackLightDirection|float3|-0.2, -1, 0.4|||The direction to use for the fallback light \(used only for Distant light types\)|
|rtx.fallbackLightFocusExponent|float|2|0||The focus exponent to use for the fallback light shaping \(used only for non\-Distant light types with shaping enabled\)\.|
|rtx.fallbackLightMode|int|1|||The mode to determine when to create a fallback light\.<br>Never \(0\) never creates the light, NoLightsPresent \(1\) creates the fallback light only when no lights are provided to Remix, and Always \(2\) always creates the fallback light\.<br>Primarily a debugging feature, users should create their own lights via the Remix workflow rather than relying on this feature to provide lighting\.<br>As such, this option should be set to Never for "production" builds of Remix creations to avoid the fallback light from appearing in games unintentionally in cases where no lights exist \(which is the default behavior when set to NoLightsPresent\)\.|
|rtx.fallbackLightPositionOffset|float3|0, 0, 0|||The position offset from the camera origin to use for the fallback light \(used only for non\-Distant light types\)\.|
|rtx.fallbackLightPrimaryAxis|float3|0, 0, -1|||The primary axis to use for the fallback light shaping \(used only for non\-Distant light types\)\.|
|rtx.fallbackLightRadiance|float3|1.6, 1.8, 2|0, 0, 0||The radiance to use for the fallback light \(used across all light types\)\.|
|rtx.fallbackLightRadius|float|5|0||The radius to use for the fallback light \(used only for Sphere light types\)\.|
|rtx.fallbackLightType|int|0|||The light type to use for the fallback light\. Determines which other fallback light options are used\.|
|rtx.fallbackLightVolumetricRadianceScale|float|1|0|65504|Scales the fallback light's contribution to volumetric lighting only, for all fallback light types\. Its surface lighting is unchanged\. 0 removes the fallback light from volumetric lighting\.|
|rtx.faultNotice.enable|bool|False|||Shows a notice on top of the game when a problem is detected: a likely conflict with another mod, a broken install, or a crash in the previous session\. The notice names the log and crash dump folders and where to get help, and stays until the user menu is opened\.|
|rtx.fireflyFilteringLuminanceThreshold|float|1000|0||Maximum luminance threshold for the firefly filtering to clamp to\.|
|rtx.fogApplyToSky|bool|True|||Applies depth\-based fog to sky pixels that show no authored sky, so the void past the level fades into the fog\. The game's own sky draws, the sky provider and the dome light are never fogged\. Fog on geometry is unaffected\.|
|rtx.fogColorScale|float|0.25||||
|rtx.fogIgnoreSky|bool|False|||If true, sky draw calls will be skipped when searching for the D3D9 fog values\.|
|rtx.foliageRays.cullNonOpaqueFromBounce|int|-1|-1|15|Indirect rays at this bounce and later skip non\-opaque geometry instead of alpha testing it\. \-1: off\. 0: every indirect ray\. 1: from the second indirect bounce on\.<br>Alpha\-tested geometry with an opacity micromap still occludes through its opaque micro\-triangles, without the cost of repeated alpha tests\. Alpha\-tested geometry without a micromap, and blended or translucent geometry such as glass and water, is not hit by these rays at all, which brightens indirect light under foliage\. rtx\.foliageRays\.fastRayMode chooses how strict the culling is\. Applies to the SHARC update and query passes and the other indirect passes alike\.|
|rtx.foliageRays.cullNonOpaqueOnDiffuseRays|bool|False|||Applies the culling of rtx\.foliageRays\.cullNonOpaqueFromBounce to every indirect ray that leaves a diffuse lobe of an opaque surface \(diffuse reflection or transmission\), at any bounce, including the first indirect ray from the primary surface\. Specular, glossy and refraction rays keep exact alpha testing\.|
|rtx.foliageRays.fastRayMode|int|1|0|1|How the rays selected by rtx\.foliageRays\.cullNonOpaqueFromBounce and rtx\.foliageRays\.cullNonOpaqueOnDiffuseRays treat non\-opaque geometry\.<br>0 = CullNonOpaque: skips every non\-opaque surface except opaque micromap micro\-triangles\. Fastest, but cutouts without a micromap, partly covered texels, and blended and translucent surfaces stop occluding, so indirect light gets brighter\.<br>1 = Omm2State: drops the micro\-triangles the micromap bake found less than 50% covered and alpha tests the rest as usual\. Cutouts without a micromap, and blended and translucent surfaces, behave as without the option\. Needs opacity micromaps\. Only the SHARC update and query passes and the TraceRay indirect pass support it; other passes stay exact\.|
|rtx.forceCameraJitter|bool|False|||Force enables camera jitter frame to frame\.|
|rtx.forceCutoutAlpha|float|0.5|||When an object is added to the cutout textures list it will have a cutout alpha mode forced on it, using this value for the alpha test\.<br>This is meant to improve the look of some legacy mode materials using low\-resolution textures and alpha blending instead of alpha cutout as this can cause blurry halos around edges due to the difficulty of handling this sort of blending in Remix\.<br>Such objects are generally better handled with actual replacement assets using fully opaque geometry replacements or alpha cutout with higher resolution textures, so this should only be relied on until proper replacements can be authored\.|
|rtx.forceMergeAllMeshes|bool|False|||Force merges all meshes into as few BLAS as possible\.  This is generally not desirable for performance, but can be a useful debugging tool\.|
|rtx.forceStaticSceneMotionVectors|bool|False|||Forces primary screen\-space motion vectors to assume the scene geometry is static, ignoring per\-object/skinned \(virtual\) motion\. Camera motion is still fully accounted for\.This reprojects the current pixel directly into the previous frame instead of reprojecting the absolute world\-space hit position\. This is mainly for testing purposes to detect issues due to world space position reprojection errors vs using translated world space which would be the solution\.|
|rtx.frameFeatures.liveMaterialCounts|bool|False|||Decides whether a frame needs thin\-film, ray portal and thin\-opaque shading from the materials still in use, instead of from whether such a material was ever created this session\. Lets the slim shader variants return once those materials are gone\. The image is unchanged\. \[FrameFeatures\] in the log shows the result\.|
|rtx.frameFeatures.validate|bool|False|||Diagnostic: forces the full G\-buffer, direct and indirect shaders and records which features the frame's pixels actually used\. Logs \[FrameFeatures\] VIOLATION when a pixel used a feature the frame was not flagged for, which means a slim shader variant would have left out code the frame needed\. Costs the savings of the slim variants while on\. Run it once per game to check the slim variants are safe\.|
|rtx.frameGenerationType|int|0|||The frame generation technology, and the control that enables it\. 0: None \(off\), 1: DLSS \(NVIDIA DLSS Frame Generation\), 2: FSR \(AMD FSR 3 Frame Generation\)\. This sets rtx\.dlfg\.enable and rtx\.fsrfg\.enable, so do not set those directly\.<br>Environment variable: DXVK\_FRAMEGEN\_TYPE|
|rtx.frameRateLimit|int|0||1000|Caps the frame rate at this many frames per second\. 0 turns the cap off\.<br>The cap holds the game before it starts its next frame, which keeps input latency low\. With Reflex enabled the Reflex limiter does the waiting\. Games running through the 32\-bit bridge wait in the game process instead\. Frame generation adds generated frames on top of the cap\.|
|rtx.freeCameraInvertY|bool|False|||Invert free camera pitch direction\.|
|rtx.freeCameraSpeed|float|200|||Free camera speed \[GameUnits/s\]\.|
|rtx.freeCameraTurningSpeed|float|1|||Free camera turning speed \(applies to keyboard, not mouse\) \[radians/s\]\.|
|rtx.fsr.preset|int|2|||The FSR upscaling preset, which trades quality for performance\. 0: Ultra Performance \(3x\), 1: Performance \(2x\), 2: Balanced \(1\.7x\), 3: Quality \(1\.5x\), 4: Native AA \(1x\), 5: Custom \(uses rtx\.resolutionScale\)\.|
|rtx.fsrfg.debugOverlay|bool|False|||Draws the FidelityFX frame generation debug overlay: tear lines, pacing lines and a red bar on frames generated after a reset\.|
|rtx.fsrfg.enable|bool|False|||Enables FSR 3 frame generation, which inserts interpolated frames to raise the frame rate\. Set rtx\.frameGenerationType instead of this option\.<br>Environment variable: RTX\_FSRFG\_ENABLE|
|rtx.fsrfg.interpolateHud|bool|False|||Interpolates the whole presented image, HUD included, instead of drawing the current HUD over the generated frame\. When off, every pixel that differs from the Remix output counts as HUD and repeats the real frame\. Turn it on when a game changes the whole image after Remix draws it, for example with a gamma ramp, a post\-process pass or a scaled back buffer\.|
|rtx.fusedWorldViewMode|int|0|||Set if game uses a fused World\-View transform matrix\.|
|rtx.geometry.optimizeAnimatedTexcoords|bool|False|||Updates UV\-only animation of compatible fixed\-function geometry without a BLAS refit, which saves GPU time\. Shader capture, skinning, smooth normals and active opacity micromaps keep the refit\. Turn it off to always refit animated geometry\.|
|rtx.geometryHashMemoSelfCheckFrames|int|0|||Diagnostic for the geometry hash memo: every this many frames, every memo hit is hashed again in full and compared with the stored value\. A mismatch, which means a buffer write did not update the buffer's content version, is logged as \[GeometryHashMemoCheck\] \(the first 20\) and counted as memoSelfCheckMismatch in \[PerfD3D9\]\. The fresh hash is used on those frames\. 0 = off\.|
|rtx.gpuStallTimeoutSeconds|float|20|||GPU watchdog: when a command list has not finished after this many seconds, logs \[GpuStall\] and reports a lost device, so the GPU crash handling runs \(crash dumps, crash report and exit\) instead of the game hanging silently\. 0 = wait forever\.|
|rtx.gpuStallWarnSeconds|float|1|||Logs a \[GpuStall\] line with the frame and the contents of the command list when a GPU fence wait or the present frame\-latency wait takes longer than this, in seconds\. Repeats every 5 seconds while stalled\. 0 = off\.|
|rtx.graph.enable|bool|True|||Enable graph loading\.  If disabled, all graphs will be unloaded, losing any state\.|
|rtx.graph.pauseGraphUpdates|bool|False|||Pause graph updating\.  If enabled, graphs logic will not be updated, but graph state will be retained\.|
|rtx.graphicsPreset|int|5|||Overall rendering preset, higher presets result in higher image quality, lower presets result in better performance\.<br>Environment variable: DXVK\_GRAPHICS\_PRESET\_TYPE|
|rtx.gui.backgroundAlpha|float|1|0|1|A value controlling the alpha of the GUI background\.|
|rtx.gui.compactGui|bool|False|||A setting to toggle between compact and spacious GUI modes\.|
|rtx.gui.enableExternalPresenter|bool|False|||Enables GUI rendering for external presenters\.|
